# Keyless Access System Symptom Troubleshooting - Cannot select ON mode with keyless access and with the keyless remote touching the engine start/stop switch (5-door with Electric Steering Lock) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

- Keyless access system check 1 -1. Do this procedure 10 times: Turn the vehicle to the ON mode while turning the steering wheel to the left and to the right. Does the system change to ON mode? YES Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for loose or poor connection. NO Go to step 2.

-1. Do this procedure 10 times: Turn the vehicle to the ON mode while turning the steering wheel to the left and to the right.

Does the system change to ON mode?

YES

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for loose or poor connection.

NO

Go to step 2.

- Keyless access system check 2 -1. Touch the engine start/stop switch with the keyless remote. The buttons on the keyless remote should be facing you. -2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode while turning the steering wheel to the left and to the right. Does the system change to ON mode? YES Go to keyless access system symptom troubleshooting "Cannot select ON mode with keyless access, but can select ON mode with the keyless remote touching the engine start/stop switch" . NO Go to step 3.

-1. Touch the engine start/stop switch with the keyless remote. The buttons on the keyless remote should be facing you.

-2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode while turning the steering wheel to the left and to the right.

Does the system change to ON mode?

YES

Go to keyless access system symptom troubleshooting "Cannot select ON mode with keyless access, but can select ON mode with the keyless remote touching the engine start/stop switch" .

NO

Go to step 3.

- Keyless access system check 3 -1. Select KEYLESS ACCESS CONTROL UNIT from the ONE-PUSH START system select menu with the HDS, and enter SELF CHECK. One-Push - KEYLESS ACCESS CONTROL UNIT - SELF CHECK Does the system communicate with the HDS? YES Go to step 4. NO Go to step 11.

-1. Select KEYLESS ACCESS CONTROL UNIT from the ONE-PUSH START system select menu with the HDS, and enter SELF CHECK.

One-Push - KEYLESS ACCESS CONTROL UNIT - SELF CHECK

Does the system communicate with the HDS?

YES

Go to step 4.

NO

Go to step 11.

- Keyless access system check 4 -1. Reboot the HDS. -2. Select model on the HDS screen. -3. When prompted by the HDS, select YES when asked if the vehicle has keyless access system, then press and hold the engine start/stop button for at least 2 minutes. This selects the forced ON mode. Does the system change to forced ON mode? YES Go to step 5. NO Go to step 16.

-1. Reboot the HDS.

-2. Select model on the HDS screen.

-3. When prompted by the HDS, select YES when asked if the vehicle has keyless access system, then press and hold the engine start/stop button for at least 2 minutes. This selects the forced ON mode.

Does the system change to forced ON mode?

YES

Go to step 5.

NO

Go to step 16.

- PCU DTC check -1. Check for PCU DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Are any DTCs indicated? YES Troubleshoot the PCU DTCs. NO Go to step 6.

-1. Check for PCU DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC

Are any DTCs indicated?

YES

Troubleshoot the PCU DTCs.

NO

Go to step 6.

- Keyless access DTC check -1. Check for keyless access control unit DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Are any DTCs indicated? YES Troubleshoot the keyless access control unit DTCs. NO Go to step 7.

-1. Check for keyless access control unit DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC

Are any DTCs indicated?

YES

Troubleshoot the keyless access control unit DTCs.

NO

Go to step 7.

- Backup DTC check -1. Check for backup control unit DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Are any DTCs indicated? YES Troubleshoot the backup control unit DTCs. NO Go to step 8.

-1. Check for backup control unit DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC

Are any DTCs indicated?

YES

Troubleshoot the backup control unit DTCs.

NO

Go to step 8.

- Keyless access system check 5 -1. Do the keyless access remote check using the all keyless remotes . Are the all keyless remotes OK? YES Go to step 9. NO Replace the keyless remote and recheck.

-1. Do the keyless access remote check using the all keyless remotes .

Are the all keyless remotes OK?

YES

Go to step 9.

NO

Replace the keyless remote and recheck.

- Keyless access system check 6 -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2.

- Keyless access system check 1 -1. Do this procedure 10 times: Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. Does the system change to ON mode? YES Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for loose or poor connection. NO Go to step 2.

NO Go to step 3.

-1. Touch the engine start/stop switch with the keyless remote. The buttons on the keyless remote should be facing you.

-2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

YES

YES

Go to step 4.

NO

Go to step 10.

When prompted by the HDS, select YES when asked if the vehicle has keyless access system, then press and hold the engine start/stop button for at least 2 minutes. This selects the forced ON mode. Does the system change to forced ON mode? YES Go to step 5. NO Go to step 15.

-1. Reboot the HDS.

-2. Select model on the HDS screen.

-3. When prompted by the HDS, select YES when asked if the vehicle has keyless access system, then press and hold the engine start/stop button for at least 2 minutes. This selects the forced ON mode.

YES

NO

Go to step 15.

# Keyless Access System Symptom Troubleshooting - Security indicator blinks

- Keyless access system check 1 -1. Select KEYLESS ACCESS CONTROL UNIT from the ONE-PUSH START system select menu with HDS, and enter KEYLESS ACCESS SYSTEM INFORMATION. One-Push - KEYLESS ACCESS CONTROL UNIT- Smart Information Do the SYSTEM CHECK 1 and 2 with the HDS. Is N-1 OK indicated? YES Go to step 2. NO Troubleshoot the keyless access system according to the result of the SYSTEM CHECK 1 and 2.

Is N-1 OK indicated?

YES

Go to step 2.

NO

Troubleshoot the keyless access system according to the result of the SYSTEM CHECK 1 and 2.

- Keyless access system check 2 -1. With driver's power window open and all the doors closed, turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Remove all the keyless remotes from inside the vehicle. Hold them outside the open driver's window, at least 20 cm (8.0 in) away from the vehicle. -3. Press the engine start/stop switch button. The MID should request the keyless remote to touch the engine start/stop button. The LED on the engine start/stop button will flash for up to 30 seconds. -4. Touch the engine start/stop switch with the keyless remote. The buttons on the keyless remote should be facing you. The LED on the engine start/stop button will turn solid when detected. -5. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -6. Select BACKUP CONTROL UNIT from the ONE-PUSH START system select menu with the HDS, and enter SYSTEM INFORMATION. One-Push - BACKUP CONTROL UNIT - System Information Do the SYSTEM CHECK with the HDS. Is N-1 OK indicated? YES Go to step 3. NO Troubleshoot the keyless access system according to the result of the SYSTEM CHECK.

-3. Press the engine start/stop switch button. The MID should request the keyless remote to touch the engine start/stop button. The LED on the engine start/stop button will flash for up to 30 seconds.

-4. Touch the engine start/stop switch with the keyless remote. The buttons on the keyless remote should be facing you. The LED on the engine start/stop button will turn solid when detected.

NOTE:

NO: Without electric steering lock Go to keyless access system symptom troubleshooting "Cannot select ON mode with keyless access and with the keyless remote touching the engine start/stop switch" . NO: With electric steering lock Go to keyless access system symptom troubleshooting "Cannot select ON mode with keyless access and with the keyless remote touching the engine start/stop switch" .

YES

Go to keyless access system symptom troubleshooting "Cannot select ON mode with keyless access and with the keyless remote touching the engine start/stop switch" .

DTC Description | DTC

YES

Troubleshoot the keyless access control unit DTCs.

NO

Go to step 3.

# Keyless Access System Symptom Troubleshooting - The doors will not unlock or lock with the keyless remote, but will unlock or lock with the door outer handles

NOTE: Front passenger's *1 is for 4-door, passenger's *2 is for 2-door.

- Door lock actuator operation check 1 -1. Remove all the keyless remotes from inside the vehicle, and close all doors. -2. Push each LOCK and UNLOCK buttons of the keyless remote, at least 10 times. Do the door lock actuators work normally? YES Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for loose or poor connections. NO Go to step 2.

-1. Remove all the keyless remotes from inside the vehicle, and close all doors.

-2. Push each LOCK and UNLOCK buttons of the keyless remote, at least 10 times.

Do the door lock actuators work normally?

YES

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for loose or poor connections.

NO

NO

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

Does the system change to ON mode?

YES

Go to step 5.

NO

Go to step 4.

- Keyless access system check 3 -1. With driver's power window open and all of the doors closed, turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Remove all the keyless remotes from inside the vehicle. Hold them outside the open driver's window, at least 20 cm (8.0 in) away from the vehicle. -3. Press the engine start/stop switch button. The MID should request the keyless remote to touch the engine start/stop button. The LED on the engine start/stop button will flash for up to 30 seconds. -4. Touch the engine start/stop switch with the keyless remote. The buttons on the keyless remote should be facing you. Does the system change to ON mode? YES Go to keyless access system symptom troubleshooting "Cannot select ON mode with keyless access, but can select ON mode with the keyless remote touching the engine start/stop switch" . NO: Without electric steering lock Go to keyless access system symptom troubleshooting "Cannot select ON mode with keyless access and with the keyless remote touching the engine start/stop switch" . NO: With electric steering lock Go to keyless access system symptom troubleshooting "Cannot select ON mode with keyless access and with the keyless remote touching the engine start/stop switch" .

# Keyless/Power Door Locks/Security System Symptom Troubleshooting - AUTO DOOR UNLOCK does not work (2-door) (2016 2017 2018 2019 2020)

NOTE:

- Before testing, make sure the No. A1-5 (30 A) *2, No. A2-2 (30 A) *2/(50 A) *1, and No. A18 (10 A) fuses in the under-hood fuse/relay box are OK.

- Check if Auto Door Unlock feature selected OFF, under MID customized settings for each remote transmitter: Driver 1 and 2. Make sure to test operation of each Auto Door Unlock setting to check function.

- Before testing, make sure the No. B9 (10 A), No. B11 (5 A), No. B12 (10 A), No. B15 (20 A), No. B16 (20 A), No. B25 (10 A), No. B26 (10 A), No. B28 (20 A), and No. B39 (10 A) fuses in the under-dash fuse/relay box are OK.

- There are two pairs of fuses in the same circuit (No. B25 and No. B39 fuses, No. B12 and No. B26 fuses). If one fuse is blown, make sure to check the other fuse in the same circuit. If necessary, replace the blown fuse(s). *1: Without keyless access system *2: With keyless access system

*1: Without keyless access system

*2: With keyless access system

YES

NO

NO

- Open wire check (+B F/BOX MAIN2 line) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Measure the voltage between test points 1 and 2. # Millimeter Wave Radar

In general, the millimeter wave radar is mounted directly behind or below the front Honda emblem. On some models, it is mounted in the lower center area of the front bumper lower grille or to the side, behind the front bumper side trim.
````

# Removal and Installation

SRS components are located in this area. Review the SRS component locations and the precautions and procedures before doing repairs or service.

NOTE: How to read the torque specifications .

- 12 Volt Battery Terminal - Disconnect NOTE: Wait at least 3 minutes before starting work.

NOTE: Wait at least 3 minutes before starting work.

- Driver's Dashboard Lower Cover - Remove

- Passenger's Dashboard Undercover - Remove

- Both A-Pillar Trims - Remove

- Both Kick Panels - Remove

- Steering Column - Remove

- Shift Lever - Remove (CVT)

- Shift Lever Housing - Remove (M/T)

- Rear Heater Joint Duct - Remove

- Wiper Linkage Assembly - Remove

- Center Console Support Bracket - Remove (For Some Models)

- Under-Dash Fuse/Relay Box Bolt - Remove

- Brake Pedal Position Switch - Remove

- Dashboard/Steering Hanger Beam - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Driver's side 1. Disconnect the connectors (A) at the A-pillar. 2. Remove the harness clip (B). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 3. Disconnect the connectors (A) and remove the clips (B). NOTE: The positions and number of connectors vary depending on model type. Fig 1: Dashboard/Steering Hanger Beam Bolts With Torque Specifications Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 4. Remove the bolts (A) from under the dashboard. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Center console area 5. For some models: Disconnect the connectors (A). 6. Remove the harness clips (B). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 7. Remove the bolts (A) from under the dashboard. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Passenger's side 8. Disconnect the connector (A) at the A-pillar. 9. Remove the harness clip (B). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 10. Disconnect the connectors (A). 11. With stereo amplifier: Disconnect the connectors (B), and remove the harness clip (C). Passenger's Side Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Engine Compartment Side Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 12. Loosen the special bolts (A) until they disengage from the threads on the hanger beam brackets (B), and engage the inside threads of the collar bolts (C). The thread lock on the special bolts makes the special bolts and the collar bolts turn together. 13. Continue to loosen the special bolts to turn the collar bolts into the fixed space adjusters (D) until the collar bolts engage the adjuster brackets. This creates a gap (E) between the collar bolts and the body. 14. Remove the special bolts. Passenger's Side Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Engine Compartment Side Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 15. Check that the collar bolts (A) are seated in the fixed space adjusters (B). NOTE: If either collar bolt was not seated, replace the special bolts. Fig 2: Dashboard/Steering Hanger Beam Bolts With Torque Specifications - Driver's Side Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 16. Remove the bolts (A). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 17. Lift up the dashboard/steering hanger beam (A) to release it from the guide pins (B) on the body. 18. Carefully remove the dashboard/steering hanger beam through the front door opening.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | Driver's side 1. Disconnect the connectors (A) at the A-pillar. 2. Remove the harness clip (B).

Install the parts in the reverse order of removal. NOTE: Before reinstalling the dashboard, screw the special bolt (A) into the collar bolts (B), and check that they turn together. If they do not turn together, replace the special bolts. Before reinstalling the dashboard, be sure the collar bolt on the passenger's side and the engine compartment side can be screwed/unscrewed lightly by hand, and then screw them into the space adjuster (C) fully by hand. Do not tighten them with any tools. After setting the dashboard in the body, reinstall all of the mounting bolts but do not tighten them. First tighten the driver's side mounting bolts to the specified torque. Next, tighten the special bolts. As you tighten the bolts, the collar bolts screw out of the space adjuster until they contact the body. Continue tightening the special bolts to the specified torque.

Do not tighten them with any tools. After setting the dashboard in the body, reinstall all of the mounting bolts but do not tighten them. First tighten the driver's side mounting bolts to the specified torque. Next, tighten the special bolts. As you tighten the bolts, the collar bolts screw out of the space adjuster until they contact the body. Continue tightening the special bolts to the specified torque.

- Before reinstalling the dashboard, screw the special bolt (A) into the collar bolts (B), and check that they turn together. If they do not turn together, replace the special bolts.

# Millimeter Wave Radar Removal and Installation (2019 2020 2021): Removal

NOTE: How to read the torque specifications .

- Front Bumper - Remove

- Millimeter Wave Radar - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Millimeter Wave Radar Assembly Removal 1. Disconnect the connector (A). 2. Remove the harness clip (B). 3. Remove the millimeter wave radar assembly (C). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Millimeter Wave Radar Subassembly Removal NOTE: The millimeter wave radar cannot be disassembled. 1. Rotate the three clips (A) counterclockwise. 2. Remove the millimeter wave radar subassembly (B) from the clips. 3. Remove the clips from the radar bracket (C).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Millimeter Wave Radar Assembly Removal

1. Disconnect the connector (A).

2. Remove the harness clip (B).

3. Remove the millimeter wave radar assembly (C).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Millimeter Wave Radar Subassembly Removal

NOTE: The millimeter wave radar cannot be disassembled.

1. Rotate the three clips (A) counterclockwise.

2. Remove the millimeter wave radar subassembly (B) from the clips.

3. Remove the clips from the radar bracket (C).
````

# Millimeter Wave Radar Removal and Installation (2019 2020 2021): Installation

NOTE: How to read the torque specifications .

- Millimeter Wave Radar - Install Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Millimeter Wave Radar Subassembly Installation 1. Install new clips (A) on the radar bracket (B). 2. Install the millimeter wave radar subassembly (C) on the clips. 3. Rotate the three clips clockwise to secure the millimeter wave radar subassembly to the radar bracket. 4. Measure the distance from D to the radar bracket and E to the radar bracket. Both measured values should be within the range of the standard value. Standard: approximately 44.8 mm (1.764 in) Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Millimeter Wave Radar Assembly Installation 1. Install the millimeter wave radar assembly (A). 2. Install the harness clip (B). 3. Connect the connector (C) while holding the millimeter wave radar assembly. NOTE: Applying excessive force may deform the radar bracket.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Millimeter Wave Radar Subassembly Installation

1. Install new clips (A) on the radar bracket (B).

2. Install the millimeter wave radar subassembly (C) on the clips.

3. Rotate the three clips clockwise to secure the millimeter wave radar subassembly to the radar bracket.

4. Measure the distance from D to the radar bracket and E to the radar bracket. Both measured values should be within the range of the standard value.

Standard: | approximately 44.8 mm (1.764 in)

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Millimeter Wave Radar Assembly Installation

1. Install the millimeter wave radar assembly (A).

2. Install the harness clip (B).

3. Connect the connector (C) while holding the millimeter wave radar assembly.

NOTE: Applying excessive force may deform the radar bracket.

- Front Bumper - Install

- Millimeter Wave Radar - Update

- Millimeter Wave Radar - Aim

NOTE: The millimeter wave radar must be re-aimed if the millimeter wave radar is removed or replaced: the vertical radar aiming mechanically done using the level included in the alignment set. When the millimeter wave radar is adjusted on a floor surface that has more than one degree of horizontal slope (drainage slope), the vertical radar aiming cannot be adjusted correctly. Check the levelness of the floor surface, but if there is a continuous slope, then adjust the angle of millimeter wave radar so it is parallel to the floor surface. Lay a ruler or other flat straight object on the floor surface and put the level on top to confirm the levelness of the floor surface. Have the driver sit in the driver's seat. Clean the surface of the millimeter wave radar. The 3.5 mm hex socket driver used in this procedure is included in the adaptive speed control alignment tool SPX 10243 or adjuster, radar 3.5 mm 07AAA-TGVA100. 1. Put the level on the pad of the millimeter wave radar. 2. Turn the vertical radar aiming bolt (A) using the 3.5 mm hex socket driver (B), and adjust the angle of millimeter wave radar so that the air bubble in the level is centered. NOTE: To correctly confirm the bubble position in the level, have a look from the side. 3. Remove the level.

# RDM Switch Removal, Installation, and Test: Test

- RDM OFF Switch - Test Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. RDM OFF Switch Internal Resistance Check 1. Measure the resistance between RDM OFF switch 10P connector terminals No. 9 and No. 10 according to the table. If there is no resistance in one or more positions, replace the RDM OFF switch. Position Resistance OFF (RELEASED) About 13.5 kΩ ON (PRESSED) About 3.5 kΩ Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. RDM OFF Switch Indicator Drive Circuit Check 1. Check the LED for illumination when connecting battery power and ground to the switch terminals according to the table. NOTE: Note this important operating characteristic; diode bias causes a diode to fully conduct electricity in one direction (forward), while not at all in the opposite direction (reverse). If the results are not as specified, replace the RDM OFF switch. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. RDM OFF Switch Illumination Circuit Check 1. Check the LED for illumination when connecting battery power and ground to the switch terminals according to the table. NOTE: Note this important operating characteristic; diode bias causes a diode to fully conduct electricity in one direction (forward), while not at all in the opposite direction (reverse). If the results are not as specified, replace the RDM OFF switch.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

RDM OFF Switch Internal Resistance Check

1. Measure the resistance between RDM OFF switch 10P connector terminals No. 9 and No. 10 according to the table.

- If there is no resistance in one or more positions, replace the RDM OFF switch.

Position | Resistance

OFF (RELEASED) | About 13.5 kΩ

ON (PRESSED) | About 3.5 kΩ

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

RDM OFF Switch Indicator Drive Circuit Check

1. Check the LED for illumination when connecting battery power and ground to the switch terminals according to the table.

NOTE: Note this important operating characteristic; diode bias causes a diode to fully conduct electricity in one direction (forward), while not at all in the opposite direction (reverse).

If the results are not as specified, replace the RDM OFF switch.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

RDM OFF Switch Illumination Circuit Check

1. Check the LED for illumination when connecting battery power and ground to the switch terminals according to the table.

NOTE: Note this important operating characteristic; diode bias causes a diode to fully conduct electricity in one direction (forward), while not at all in the opposite direction (reverse).

If the results are not as specified, replace the RDM OFF switch.

# Before Aiming Setting (Center Muffler Type) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021): Adjustment

- Before Aiming - Set NOTE: Aiming should be done in a well-lighted area. There should be no bright objects at the back of the target (sunlight, windows, illumination, or reflectors) or designs similar to the target pattern. Make sure the suspension has not been modified. Make sure all the tire sizes are correct and that their pressure is correct. Make sure the fuel tank is full. Remove all cargo from the vehicle except the tool kit and the spare tire. Prepare these items: Tape measure and ruler Felt-tip pen Tape String Plumb bob Measure the Lines Along the Sides of the Vehicle 1. Measure 76.2 mm (3.00 in) from the edge of the centering stand, and mark a new point Z1 that intersects the wheel's center-line.. NOTE: Repeat this step for each wheel (Z2, Y1, and Y2) Set the Cord Along the Center-Line of the Vehicle: NOTE: When installing a cord along the center-line of the vehicle, install the cord using these items as reference points. The reference items may vary by vehicle: - Front and rear jacking brackets - Towing hook - Front emblem - Scribe lines of the front subframe part - Center plastic bumper clip - Plastic clips of the rear bumper lower trim When installing an aiming target on the rear of the vehicle, use the centering stand in the rear. Place the centering stand under the center bumper clip (B) so that the pointer is at the center of the clip, then mark new a point X at the center-line of the centering stand. 4. Using the two clips (C) and (D) securing the rear bumper lower trim, suspend a plumb bob from the center of each clip and make points U and V on the floor surface where the plumb bob comes in contact with the floor. Place a string so that it passes over points U and V on the floor surface and secure the string with tape. 5. Using a tape measure, measure the distance between points U and V and determine the center of the two points. Mark this point as W. Place the center-line of the centering stand at point W. For targets at the front of the vehicle 6. Place one end of the cord on point W and secure it to the floor so it does not move. NOTE: Lightly pull on the cord to make sure that it is secured and apply additional tape as necessary. 7. Place the center-line of the centering stand at point X and run the cord through it. 8. Place the cord in front of the centering stand until you are ready to the set up the target position for the applicable aiming procedure. For targets at the rear of the vehicle 9. Attach one end of the cord and position the gas strut holder between the center bumper clip and point X on the floor surface. 10. Place the center-line of the centering stand at point W and run the cord through it. 11. Place the cord in front of the centering stand until you are ready to the set up the target position for the applicable aiming procedure.

# Before Aiming Setting (Except Center Muffler Type): Adjustment

- Before Aiming - Set NOTE: Aiming should be done in a well-lighted area. There should be no bright objects at the back of the target (sunlight, windows, illumination, or reflectors) or designs similar to the target pattern. Make sure the suspension has not been modified. Make sure all the tire sizes are correct and that their pressure is correct. Make sure the fuel tank is full. Remove all cargo from the vehicle except the tool kit and the spare tire. Make sure the steering wheel is pointed straight ahead, and do not turn it after setting the vehicle. Shift the shift lever to P (CVT) or neutral (M/T) position, and apply the parking brake. When marking on the floor surface, use suitable tape to mark the exact locations. Prepare these items: Tape measure and ruler Felt-tip pen Tape String Measure the Lines Along the Sides of the Vehicle 1. Find the center (A) of one wheel using the centering stand and mark it. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. Measure 76.2 mm (3.00 in) from the edge of the centering stand, and mark a new spot Z1 that intersects the wheel's center-line. NOTE: Repeat this step for each wheel (Z2, Y1, and Y2). Set the Cord Along the Center-Line of the Vehicle NOTE: When installing a cord along the center-line of the vehicle, install the cord using these items as reference points. The reference items may vary by vehicle. - Front and rear jacking brackets - Towing hook - Front emblem - Scribe lines of the front subframe part - Center bumper clip on the bulkhead When installing an aiming target on the rear of the vehicle, use the centering stand in the rear. When installing an aiming target on the front of the vehicle, use the centering stand in the front. 3. Place the centering stand under the center bumper clip (B), then mark new a spot X, at the center-line of the centering stand. 4. Place the centering stand under the center of the rear jack up bracket, then mark a new spot W at the center-line of the centering stand. For targets at the front of the vehicle 5. Position the gas strut holder of the alignment set between the floor and the rear jack up bracket at mark W. 6. Place the center-line of the centering stand at mark X and run the cord through it. 7. Place the cord in front of the centering stand until you are ready to the set up the target position for the applicable aiming procedure. For targets at the rear of the vehicle 8. Position the gas strut holder of the alignment set between the floor and the center bumper clip at mark X. 9. Place the center-line of the centering stand at mark W and run the cord through it. 10. Place the cord in front of the centering stand until you are ready to the set up the target position for the applicable aiming procedure.

````text
# Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming (2019 2020 2021): Adjustment

NOTE:

- The millimeter wave radar must be re-aimed if the millimeter wave radar is removed or replaced. The ACC indicator comes on if the aiming process is not completed correctly:

- - The millimeter wave radar has a self-diagnosis function and if the mounting angle of the millimeter wave radar is outside of the horizontal auto-aiming range, it sends the failure information to the multipurpose camera unit.

- - The millimeter wave radar has a self-diagnosis function and if the mounting angle of the millimeter wave radar is outside of the horizontal auto-aiming range, it sends the failure information to the multipurpose camera unit.

The millimeter wave radar has a self-diagnosis function and if the mounting angle of the millimeter wave radar is outside of the horizontal auto-aiming range, it sends the failure information to the multipurpose camera unit.

- If DTCs are indicated, do the indicated DTCs first. If the DTCs are not repaired, you cannot complete the millimeter wave radar aiming procedure. However, if you have the following DTC, proceed with the aiming procedure.

- - Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming Incomplete: DTC P2583-54

- - Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming Incomplete: DTC P2583-54

Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming Incomplete: DTC P2583-54

- If the vehicle you are aiming was involved in a collision or if you suspect the alignment is out of specifications, you must perform a wheel alignment prior to aiming the millimeter wave radar.

- Conditions for the aiming environment:

- - Aiming should be on level ground in a wide open space to avoid errors caused by reflecting objects. - Amount of space require in front of the vehicle: 10 m (33 ft) measured from the front bumper 5 m (16 ft) in width 3 m (10 ft) in height, measured from the floor

- - Aiming should be on level ground in a wide open space to avoid errors caused by reflecting objects.

Aiming should be on level ground in a wide open space to avoid errors caused by reflecting objects.

- - Amount of space require in front of the vehicle: 10 m (33 ft) measured from the front bumper 5 m (16 ft) in width 3 m (10 ft) in height, measured from the floor

Amount of space require in front of the vehicle:

10 m (33 ft) measured from the front bumper

5 m (16 ft) in width

3 m (10 ft) in height, measured from the floor

- The vehicle must be parked on a horizontal floor in order to adjust the vertical radar aiming correctly. Check the levelness of the floor surface, but if there is a continuous slope, then adjust the angle of the millimeter wave radar so it is parallel to the floor surface. Lay a ruler or other flat straight object on the floor surface and put the level on top to confirm the levelness of the floor surface.

- When marking on the floor surface, use suitable tape to mark the exact locations.

Prepare these items:

- HDS

- Tape measure and ruler

- Felt-tip pen

- Tape

- String

- Plumb bob

- Before Aiming - Set Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. NOTE: Make sure the steering wheel is pointed straight ahead, and do not turn it after setting up the vehicle. Marking of the Target Position: 1. Make points (Z1, Y1, Z2, and Y2) and set the cord along the center-line of the vehicle . 2. Place a string (A) between point Z1 and point Y1, and tape both ends of the string on the floor surface. NOTE: Repeat this step for the opposite side for points Z2 and Y2. 3. Measure and make a new point B on the floor surface in front of the vehicle, 4, 000 mm (157.48 in) from point Z1. 4. Pull one end of the string to point B and tape it to the floor surface. NOTE: Repeat steps 3 and 4 for the opposite side to mark a new point C. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 5. Stretch a string (A) between point B and point C, and tape both ends of the string on the floor surface. 6. Route the cord (E) through the collar and place it on the stand set. 7. Use the stand set to stretch the cord of the center-line, and mark a new point D where the string crosses with the cord. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 8. On the string (A), mark a new point F from point D. NOTE: Inspect the 11 character of the vehicle's VIN number and use the below table to determine which manufacturing plant and what value to use.
````

- Millimeter Wave Radar - Aim Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Connect the HDS . NOTE: Keep the aiming target away from the vehicle until you are instructed in a later step. 2. Check for DTCs with the HDS. NOTE: Troubleshoot any DTCs first before proceeding. If DTC P2583-54 is indicated, proceed with the aiming procedure. 3. In the DRIVING SUPPORT menu, select INTEGRATED DRIVER SUPPORT system, then ADJUSTMENT, then select Radar Aiming. 4. Press ENTER on the HDS to enter the aiming mode. NOTE: The ACC, LKAS, and RDM indicators (amber) blink when aiming mode is activated. 5. The MID will display NO TARGET. 6. Make sure the MID indicates NO TARGET on the display. NOTE: If NO TARGET is not indicated, the stand set is placed in front of the vehicle or there is an unwanted radar reflection in front of the vehicle in the range of the radar. Make sure the stand set is set aside away from the range of the radar until you are instructed to place it in front of the vehicle. If you are still getting an unwanted radar reflection, stack tires about 1.5 m (4.9 ft) in height in front of either the immovable object, such as a pole, or in front of any object causing the unwanted reflections. The tires will block the radar reflections. Make sure NO TARGET appears on the MID. If it still does not appear, move the vehicle to a different location until NO TARGET appears on the MID. 7. Place the stand set on the point (E). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 8. Press ENTER on the HDS to calculate the alignment angles. 9. Check the vertical and horizontal alignment angles on the HDS display. If the angles displayed on the HDS are out of the specified angle range, turn the adjustment bolt using the 3.5 mm hex socket driver (A) as necessary (counterclockwise or clockwise) to bring the displayed angle to the specified angle range. Watch the HDS display while performing the adjustment as the values will change. Specified Angle: Millimeter wave angle Specified angle range Horizontal 0 0.1 deg.
````

Millimeter wave angle | Specified angle range

- When checking the angle on the HDS screen and recording the calculation angle, do not enter the zone where it will affect the radar angle calculation.

- A value of 12.8 degrees on the HDS display indicates that the millimeter wave radar is being obstructed and cannot receive the radar wave.

- If the value of 12.8 degrees remains on the HDS screen for 15 seconds continuously, the aiming will stop.

- If the specified angle cannot be obtained with the adjustment bolts, confirm the millimeter wave radar mounting position.

NOTE: If the failure code and the message in the following list are displayed, solve the problem, and then perform the millimeter wave radar aiming again.

Failure codes and messages:

Failure code (on the MID) | Failure message (on the HDS) | How to solve

120 | Multiple Aiming Target Detected | Remove all reflective objects except the correct special tools.

122 | The Distance between Radar and Aiming Target is Out of Specification | Set the specified distance to the aiming target.

124 | Detected Some Obstacles | Remove all obstacles.

125 | There is no Aiming Target | Set the aiming target at the correct position.

129 | A Vehicle Speed is not 0 km/h | Stop the vehicle completely.

132 | Internal Software Error | Update the millimeter wave radar .

134 | High Temperature Error | Cool down the millimeter wave radar to the proper operating temperature.

135 | Internal Hardware Error | Replace the millimeter wave radar .

# Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming (2/4-door, Except Japan Production) (2016 2017 2018): Adjustment

NOTE:

- The millimeter wave radar must be re-aimed if the millimeter wave radar is removed or replaced. The ACC indicator comes on if the aiming process is not completed correctly. If DTCs are indicated, do the indicated DTCs first. If the DTCs are not repaired, you cannot complete the millimeter wave radar aiming procedure:

- - Two kinds of aiming procedures must be performed for the radar to be re-aimed: the vertical radar aiming mechanically done using a level and the horizontal radar aiming electrically done using an HDS. - The millimeter wave radar has a self-diagnosis function and if the mounting angle of the millimeter wave radar is outside of the horizontal auto-aiming range, it stores the failure information to the millimeter wave radar. - Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming Incomplete: DTC P2583-54 - Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming Error: DTC P2583-64

- - Two kinds of aiming procedures must be performed for the radar to be re-aimed: the vertical radar aiming mechanically done using a level and the horizontal radar aiming electrically done using an HDS.

Two kinds of aiming procedures must be performed for the radar to be re-aimed: the vertical radar aiming mechanically done using a level and the horizontal radar aiming electrically done using an HDS.

- - The millimeter wave radar has a self-diagnosis function and if the mounting angle of the millimeter wave radar is outside of the horizontal auto-aiming range, it stores the failure information to the millimeter wave radar.

The millimeter wave radar has a self-diagnosis function and if the mounting angle of the millimeter wave radar is outside of the horizontal auto-aiming range, it stores the failure information to the millimeter wave radar.

- - Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming Incomplete: DTC P2583-54

Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming Incomplete: DTC P2583-54

- - Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming Error: DTC P2583-64

Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming Error: DTC P2583-64

- If the vehicle you are aiming was involved in a collision or if you suspect the alignment is out of specifications, you must perform a wheel alignment prior to aiming the millimeter wave radar.

- Conditions for the aiming environment:

- - Aiming should be on level ground in a wide open space to avoid errors caused by reflecting objects. - Amount of space require in front of the vehicle: 10 m (33 ft) measured from the front bumper 4 m (13 ft) in width 1.5 m (4.9 ft) in height, measured from the floor surface

- - Aiming should be on level ground in a wide open space to avoid errors caused by reflecting objects.

Aiming should be on level ground in a wide open space to avoid errors caused by reflecting objects.

- - Amount of space require in front of the vehicle: 10 m (33 ft) measured from the front bumper 4 m (13 ft) in width 1.5 m (4.9 ft) in height, measured from the floor surface

Amount of space require in front of the vehicle:

10 m (33 ft) measured from the front bumper

4 m (13 ft) in width

1.5 m (4.9 ft) in height, measured from the floor surface

- Have the driver sit in the driver's seat.

- When marking on the floor surface, use suitable tape to mark the exact locations.

- Prepare these items:

- - HDS - Tape measure and ruler - Felt-tip pen - Tape - String - Mirror

- - HDS

HDS

- - Tape measure and ruler

Tape measure and ruler

- - Felt-tip pen

Felt-tip pen

Tape

- - String

String

- - Mirror

Mirror

- Millimeter Wave Radar - Vertical Radar Aim NOTE: When vertical radar aiming is completed after the millimeter wave radar is removed and installed, begin with horizontal aiming. Clean the surface of the millimeter wave radar. The 3.5 mm hex socket driver used in this procedure is included in the adaptive speed control alignment tool SPX 10243 or adjuster, radar 3.5 mm 07AAA-TGVA100. NOTE: The vertical radar aiming cannot be set correctly if the vehicle is parked on a slope with more than one degree of horizontal slope. Use the level to ensure inspection site is within the range of condition. 1. Insert a flat-tip screwdriver in the bottom hole (A) of the front inner fender, and while pushing from the inside of the radar cover (B) using a flat-tip screwdriver, release the hook (C) of the radar cover from the fog light trim using a clip remover in the numbered sequence. NOTE: When inserting a flat-tip screwdriver, wrap it with protective tape to prevent damage.
````

- Before Aiming - Set

- Millimeter Wave Radar - Horizontal Radar Aim Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. NOTE: Aiming the millimeter wave radar requires the HDS. Make sure the steering wheel is pointed straight ahead, and do not turn it after setting up the vehicle. Marking of the Target Position 1. Pull a string (A) between point Z1 and point Y1, and tape both ends of the string on the floor surface. NOTE: Repeat this step for the opposite side for points Z2 and Y2. 2. Measure and make new point B on the floor surface in front of the vehicle 4, 000 mm (157.48 in) from point Z1. NOTE: Repeat this step for the opposite side, and mark new point C on the floor surface. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 3. Set the string (A) between point B and point C, and tape both ends of the string on the floor surface. Mark new point D where the string crosses with the center-line (E). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 4. On the string (A), mark new point F (target of the left side) which is 569 mm (22.40 in) from point D. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Implementation of the Horizontal Radar Aiming 5. Connect the HDS . 6. Check for DTCs with the HDS. NOTE: If DTC P2583-54 or DTC P2583-64 is indicated, troubleshoot the indicated DTC first. 7. In the DRIVING SUPPORT MENU, select INTEGRATED DRIVER SUPPORT SYSTEM, then ADJUSTMENT, then select Radar Aiming on the HDS. 8. Press ENTER on the HDS to enter the aiming mode. 9. The MID will display NO TARGET. 10. Make sure the MID indicates NO TARGET on the display. NOTE: If NO TARGET is not indicated, there is an unwanted radar reflection in front of the vehicle in the range of the radar. If available, stack tires about 1.5 m (4.9 ft) in height in front of either the immovable object, such as a pole, or in front of any object causing the unwanted reflections. The tires will block the radar reflections. Make sure NO TARGET appears on the MID. If it still does not appear, move the vehicle to a different location until NO TARGET appears on the MID. 11. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 12. Adjust the tip of the radar aimer set so that it is aligned with the center of the millimeter wave radar surface. 13. Mark a line (Y) on the pole of the radar aimer set. 14. Install the radar cover. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 15. Mark a new line (Y1) on the pole which is 35 mm (1.38 in) above the line (Y). 16. Slide the corner reflector (A) upward to the position Y1. 17. Turn the corner reflector (A) towards the vehicle side. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 18. Position the stand set to align the hole (E) of the stand set with the point (F). Then make sure that you can see the mark through the hole. 19. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, then press ENTER on the HDS to start the horizontal aiming. 20. Confirm THE RADAR AIMING HAS BEEN COMPLETE on the HDS. After adjustment, confirm the MID, and finalize the horizontal radar aiming (end the aiming process on the HDS). NOTE: The result of horizontal radar aiming can be confirmed by the HDS or the MID. If the horizontal radar aiming is confirmed, the MID will display RADAR OK with code 100. When the target is out of range, the horizontal radar aiming is not complete. The MID will display NO TARGET. If the horizontal radar aiming failure code 120, 122, 123, or 124 is indicated, the aiming target is not set correctly. Inspect the millimeter wave radar mounting for bent or damaged parts. Verify the vertical radar aiming, and reset the aiming target if necessary. The aiming information obtained is stored in the millimeter wave radar. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Horizontal Radar Aiming Failure Codes (if the horizontal radar aiming is incomplete) NOTE: If the horizontal radar aiming is incomplete, the MID indicates a horizontal radar aiming failure code. The multipurpose camera unit does not store the horizontal radar aiming failure code and you cannot read the horizontal radar aiming failure codes with the HDS, so note the horizontal radar aiming failure code before turning the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.
````

### Full Text

````text
# Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming (4-door, Japan Production) (2018): Adjustment

NOTE:

- The millimeter wave radar must be re-aimed if the millimeter wave radar is removed or replaced. The ACC indicator comes on if the aiming process is not completed correctly.

- - Two kinds of aiming procedures must be performed for the radar to be re-aimed; the vertical radar aiming and the horizontal radar aiming, electronically done using the HDS. - The millimeter wave radar has a self-diagnosis function and if the mounting angle of the millimeter wave radar is outside of the horizontal auto-aiming range, it sends the failure information to the multipurpose camera unit.

- - Two kinds of aiming procedures must be performed for the radar to be re-aimed; the vertical radar aiming and the horizontal radar aiming, electronically done using the HDS.

Two kinds of aiming procedures must be performed for the radar to be re-aimed; the vertical radar aiming and the horizontal radar aiming, electronically done using the HDS.

- - The millimeter wave radar has a self-diagnosis function and if the mounting angle of the millimeter wave radar is outside of the horizontal auto-aiming range, it sends the failure information to the multipurpose camera unit.

The millimeter wave radar has a self-diagnosis function and if the mounting angle of the millimeter wave radar is outside of the horizontal auto-aiming range, it sends the failure information to the multipurpose camera unit.

- If DTCs are indicated, do the indicated DTCs first. If the DTCs are not repaired, you cannot complete the millimeter wave radar aiming procedure. However, if you have the following DTC, proceed with the aiming procedure.

- - Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming Incomplete: DTC P2583-54

- - Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming Incomplete: DTC P2583-54

Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming Incomplete: DTC P2583-54

- If the vehicle you are aiming was involved in a collision or if you suspect the alignment is out of specifications, you must perform a wheel alignment prior to aiming the millimeter wave radar.

- Conditions for the aiming environment:

- - Aiming should be on level ground in a wide open space to avoid errors caused by reflecting objects. - Amount of space require in front of the vehicle: 10 m (33 ft) measured from the front bumper4 m (13 ft) in width1.5 m (4.9 ft) in height, measured from the floor surface

- - Aiming should be on level ground in a wide open space to avoid errors caused by reflecting objects.

Aiming should be on level ground in a wide open space to avoid errors caused by reflecting objects.

- - Amount of space require in front of the vehicle: 10 m (33 ft) measured from the front bumper4 m (13 ft) in width1.5 m (4.9 ft) in height, measured from the floor surface

Amount of space require in front of the vehicle: 10 m (33 ft) measured from the front bumper4 m (13 ft) in width1.5 m (4.9 ft) in height, measured from the floor surface

- The vehicle must be parked on a horizontal floor in order to adjust the vertical radar aiming correctly. Check the levelness of the floor surface, but if there is a continuous slope, then adjust the angle of the millimeter wave radar so it is parallel to the floor surface. Lay a ruler or other flat straight object on the floor surface and put the level on top to confirm the levelness of the floor surface.

- When marking on the floor, use suitable tape to mark the exact locations.

Prepare these items:

- HDS

- Tape measure and ruler

- Felt-tip pen

- Tape

- String

- Plumb bob

- Before Aiming - Set 1. Insert a flat-tip screwdriver through hole (A) of the front inner fender, and while pushing the back side of the radar cover (B), release the hooks (C) using a trim tool as shown. NOTE: Wrap a flat-tip screwdriver with protective tape to prevent damage. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. Remove the radar cover from the fog light trim. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Marking of the Target Position NOTE: Make sure the steering wheel is pointed straight ahead, and do not turn it after setting up the vehicle. 3. Make points (Z1, Y1, Z2, and Y2) and set the cord along the center-line of the vehicle . NOTE: Repeat this step for the opposite side for points Z2 and Y2. 4. Place a string (A) between point Z1 and point Y1, and tape both ends of the string on the floor surface. 5. Measure and make a new point B on the floor surface in front of the vehicle 4, 000 mm (157.48 in) from the point Z1.
````

Aiming failure code (on the MID) | Cause | How to solve

120 | Two or more aiming targets exist. | Remove all reflective objects except the correct special tools, and repeat the aiming procedure.

122 | The distance of the aiming target is out of specification. | Set the distance to the aiming target 3, 850-4, 150 mm (151.57-163.39 in), and repeat the aiming procedure.

124 | The angle of the millimeter wave radar is exceeding the adjustable range or the height of the aiming target is out of the specified range. | Inspect and adjust the millimeter wave radar mounting position or the height of the aiming target to the specified height and repeat the aiming procedure.

125 | There is no aiming target. | Reset the aiming target at the correct position, and repeat the aiming procedure.

12. After adjustment, select "Enter", then go to the horizontal aiming.

### Full Text

````text
# Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming (5-door) (2017 2018): Adjustment

NOTE:

- The millimeter wave radar must be re-aimed if the millimeter wave radar is removed or replaced. The ACC indicator comes on if the aiming process is not completed correctly:

- - The millimeter wave radar has a self-diagnosis function and if the mounting angle of the millimeter wave radar is outside of the horizontal auto-aiming range, it stores the failure information to the millimeter wave radar.

- - The millimeter wave radar has a self-diagnosis function and if the mounting angle of the millimeter wave radar is outside of the horizontal auto-aiming range, it stores the failure information to the millimeter wave radar.

The millimeter wave radar has a self-diagnosis function and if the mounting angle of the millimeter wave radar is outside of the horizontal auto-aiming range, it stores the failure information to the millimeter wave radar.

- If DTCs are indicated, do the indicated DTCs first. If the DTCs are not repaired, you cannot complete the millimeter wave radar aiming procedure. However, if you have the following DTC, proceed with the aiming procedure.

- - Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming Incomplete: DTC P2583-54

- - Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming Incomplete: DTC P2583-54

Millimeter Wave Radar Aiming Incomplete: DTC P2583-54

- If the vehicle you are aiming was involved in a collision or if you suspect the alignment is out of specifications, you must perform a wheel alignment prior to aiming the millimeter wave radar.

- Conditions for the aiming environment:

- - Aiming should be on level ground in a wide open space to avoid errors caused by reflecting objects. - Amount of space require in front of the vehicle: 10 m (33 ft) measured from the front bumper 5 m (16 ft) in width 3 m (10 ft) in height, measured from the floor

- - Aiming should be on level ground in a wide open space to avoid errors caused by reflecting objects.

- The vehicle must be parked on a horizontal floor in order to adjust the vertical radar aiming correctly. Check the levelness of the floor surface, lay a ruler or other flat straight object on the floor surface and put the level on top to confirm the levelness of the floor surface.

- When marking on the floor surface, use suitable tape to mark the exact locations.

- Before Aiming - Set Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. NOTE: Make sure the steering wheel is pointed straight ahead, and do not turn it after setting up the vehicle. Marking of the Target Position: 1. Make points (Z1, Y1, Z2, and Y2) and set the cord along the center-line of the vehicle. 2. Pull a string (A) between point Z1 and point Y1, and tape both ends of the string on the floor surface. NOTE: Repeat this step for the opposite side for points Z2 and Y2. 3. Measure and make a new point B on the floor surface in front of the vehicle 4, 000 mm (157.48 in) from point Z1. NOTE: Repeat this step for the opposite side, and mark a new point C. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 4. Set the string (A) between point B and point C, and tape both ends of the string on the floor surface. Mark a new point D where the string crosses with the center-line (E). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 5. On the string (A), mark new point F which is 583 mm (22.95 in) from point D. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 6. Release the hook of the radar cover (A) from the fog light trim using a flat-tip screwdriver, then remove the radar cover. NOTE: When inserting a flat-tip screwdriver, wrap it with protective tape to prevent damage. Clean the surface of the millimeter wave radar. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 7. Align the end of the shaft to the stand set to the lower edge of the millimeter wave radar. 8. Draw a line (Y) on the pole of the stand set as shown. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 9.

- Millimeter Wave Radar - Aim Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Connect the HDS . NOTE: Keep the aiming target away from the vehicle until you are instructed in a later step. 2. Check for DTCs with the HDS. NOTE: Troubleshoot any DTCs first before proceeding. If DTC P2583-54 is indicated, proceed with the aiming procedure. 3. In the DRIVING SUPPORT menu, select INTEGRATED DRIVER SUPPORT system, then ADJUSTMENT, then select Radar Aiming and follow the screen prompts. 4. Press ENTER on the HDS to enter the aiming mode. NOTE: ACC, LKAS, CMBS and RDM indicators (amber) blink when aiming mode is activated. 5. The MID will display NO TARGET. NOTE: If the HDS screen indicates "Detected Some Obstacles", refer to the note under step 6. If the HDS screen indicates to set up the target, go to step 7. 6. Make sure the MID indicates NO TARGET on the display. NOTE: If NO TARGET is not indicated, the stand set is placed in front of the vehicle or there is an unwanted radar reflection in front of the vehicle in the range of the radar. Make sure the stand set is set aside away from the range of the radar until you are instructed to place it in front of the vehicle.

TEGRATED DRIVER SUPPORT system, then ADJUSTMENT, then select Radar Aiming and follow the screen prompts. 4. Press ENTER on the HDS to enter the aiming mode. NOTE: ACC, LKAS, CMBS and RDM indicators (amber) blink when aiming mode is activated. 5. The MID will display NO TARGET. NOTE: If the HDS screen indicates "Detected Some Obstacles", refer to the note under step 6. If the HDS screen indicates to set up the target, go to step 7. 6. Make sure the MID indicates NO TARGET on the display. NOTE: If NO TARGET is not indicated, the stand set is placed in front of the vehicle or there is an unwanted radar reflection in front of the vehicle in the range of the radar. Make sure the stand set is set aside away from the range of the radar until you are instructed to place it in front of the vehicle. If you are still getting an unwanted radar reflection, stack tires about 1.5 m (4.9 ft) in height in front of either the immovable object, such as a pole, or in front of any object causing the unwanted reflections. The tires will block the radar reflections. Make sure NO TARGET appears on the MID. If it still does not appear, move the vehicle to a different location until NO TARGET appears on the MID. 7. Place the stand set on the point (E). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 8. Press ENTER on the HDS to calculate the alignment angles. NOTE: Some early 2017 models may indicate a message of "Some Driver Assist Systems Cannot Operate: Radar Obstructed" on the MID throughout this procedure. This is normal and can be ignored. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 9. Check the vertical and horizontal alignment angles on the HDS display. If the angles displayed on the HDS are out of the specified angle range, turn the adjustment bolt using the 3.5 mm hex socket driver (A) as necessary (counterclockwise or clockwise) to bring the displayed angle to the specified angle range. Watch the HDS display while performing the adjustment as the values will change. Specified Angle: Millimeter wave angle Specified angle range Horizontal 0 0.1 deg. Vertical 0 0.1 deg. Adjustment Method: Angle shown on the HDS display Rotate the 3.5 mm hex socket driver as follows More than +0.1 deg. Counterclockwise Less than -0.1 deg. Clockwise Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. NOTE: The values on the HDS display will fluctuate as the adjustment is being performed. Wait until the values stabilize to confirm the adjustment. When checking the angle on the HDS screen and recording the calculation angle, do not enter the zone where it will affect the radar angle calculation. A value of 12.8 degrees on the HDS display indicates that the millimeter wave radar is being obstructed and cannot receive the radar wave. If the value of 12.8 degree remains on the HDS screen for 15 seconds continuously, the aiming will stop. If the specified angle cannot be obtained with the adjustment bolts, confirm the millimeter wave radar mounting position . NOTE: If the failure code and the message in the following list are displayed, solve the problem, and then perform the millimeter wave radar aiming again. Failure codes and messages: Failure code (on the MID) Failure message (on the MID) How to solve 120 Multiple Aiming Target Detected Remove all reflective objects except the correct special tools. 122 The Distance between Radar and Aiming Target is Out of Specification Set the specified distance to the aiming target. 124 Detected Some Obstacles Remove all obstacles. 125 There is no Aiming Target Set the aiming target at the correct position. 129 A Vehicle Speed is not 0 km/h Stop the vehicle completely. 132 Internal Software Error Update the millimeter wave radar. 134 High Temperature Error Cool down the millimeter wave radar to the proper operating temperature. 135 Internal Hardware Error Replace the millimeter wave radar. 10. When the angle is at the specified value, press ENTER to record the angle. NOTE: The aiming information obtained is stored in the millimeter wave radar. 11. Confirm THE RADAR AIMING HAS BEEN COMPLETED on the HDS. 12. Press ENTER on the HDS, and turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. 13. Reinstall the radar cover. NOTE: If the radar cover is damaged or cracked, replace it.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. Connect the HDS . NOTE: Keep the aiming target away from the vehicle until you are instructed in a later step. 2. Check for DTCs with the HDS. NOTE: Troubleshoot any DTCs first before proceeding.

Failure code (on the MID) | Failure message (on the MID) | How to solve

120 | Multiple Aiming Target Detected | Remove all reflective objects except the correct special tools.

122 | The Distance between Radar and Aiming Target is Out of Specification | Set the specified distance to the aiming target.

124 | Detected Some Obstacles | Remove all obstacles.

125 | There is no Aiming Target | Set the aiming target at the correct position.

129 | A Vehicle Speed is not 0 km/h | Stop the vehicle completely.

132 | Internal Software Error | Update the millimeter wave radar.

134 | High Temperature Error | Cool down the millimeter wave radar to the proper operating temperature.

135 | Internal Hardware Error | Replace the millimeter wave radar.

13. Reinstall the radar cover.

NOTE: If the radar cover is damaged or cracked, replace it.

# Multipurpose Camera Aiming (2019 2020 2021): Adjustment

NOTE:

- The multipurpose camera unit must be re-aimed if the multipurpose camera unit or windshield is removed or replaced. The LKAS and ACC indicators (amber) will blink if the aiming process is not completed correctly. If DTCs are indicated, do the indicated DTCs first. If the DTCs are not repaired, you cannot complete the multipurpose camera aiming procedure:

- - Static Camera Aiming Incomplete: DTC B2A60-54

- - Static Camera Aiming Incomplete: DTC B2A60-54

Static Camera Aiming Incomplete: DTC B2A60-54

- If the vehicle you are aiming was involved in a collision or if you suspect the alignment is out of specifications, you must perform a wheel alignment prior to aiming the multipurpose camera unit.

- Cautions for the operation environment:

- - Multipurpose camera aiming should be done on a flat place. - To aim the multipurpose camera, you need the following amount of open space in front of the vehicle: 7 m (23 ft) measured from the front bumper, 4 m (13 ft) width, and 1.5 m (4.9 ft) height. - Make sure the vehicle is not facing any direct light sources as this will affect the static camera aiming. - Make sure there are no black and white checkered or dark and light alternating patterns directly behind the targets as this will affect the static camera aiming.

- - Multipurpose camera aiming should be done on a flat place.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

- Set up the vehicle:

- - Aiming the multipurpose camera requires the HDS. - Do not put any objects on the instrument panel. - Remove all cargo from the vehicle except the tool kit and the spare tire. - Make sure that there are no stains, dust, or dirt on the lens of the multipurpose camera unit and the windshield area where the camera is mounted. - Make sure that all doors are closed. - Make sure the suspension has not been modified. - Make sure all the tire sizes are correct and that their pressure is correct. - Make sure the fuel tank is full. - Make sure the steering wheel is pointed straight ahead, and do not turn it after setting the vehicle. - Shift the shift lever to P (CVT) or neutral (M/T) position, and apply the parking brake.

# Multipurpose Camera Aiming (2/4-door) (2016 2017 2018): Adjustment

NOTE:

- The multipurpose camera unit must be re-aimed if the multipurpose camera unit or windshield is removed or replaced. The LKAS and ACC indicators (amber) will blink if the aiming process is not completed correctly. If DTCs are indicated, do the indicated DTCs first. If the DTCs are not repaired, you cannot complete the multipurpose camera aiming procedure:

- - Dynamic Camera Aiming Incomplete: DTC B2A60-52 - Static Camera Aiming Incomplete: DTC B2A60-54

- - Dynamic Camera Aiming Incomplete: DTC B2A60-52

Dynamic Camera Aiming Incomplete: DTC B2A60-52

- - Static Camera Aiming Incomplete: DTC B2A60-54

Static Camera Aiming Incomplete: DTC B2A60-54

- If the vehicle you are aiming was involved in a collision or if you suspect the alignment is out of specifications, you must perform a wheel alignment prior to aiming the multipurpose camera unit.

- Cautions for the operation environment:

- - Multipurpose camera aiming should be done on a flat place. - To aim the multipurpose camera, you need the following amount of open space in front of the vehicle: 7 m (23 ft) measured from the front bumper, 4 m (13 ft) width, and 1.5 m (4.9 ft) height. - Make sure the vehicle is not facing any direct light source as this will affect the static camera aiming. - Make sure there are no black and white checkered or dark and light alternating patterns directly behind the targets as this will affect the static camera aiming.

- - Multipurpose camera aiming should be done on a flat place.

- - Make sure there are no black and white checkered or dark and light alternating patterns directly behind the targets as this will affect the static camera aiming.

Make sure there are no black and white checkered or dark and light alternating patterns directly behind the targets as this will affect the static camera aiming.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

- Set up the vehicle:

- - Aiming the multipurpose camera requires the HDS. - Do not put any objects on the instrument panel. - Remove the all cargo from the vehicle except the tool kit and the spare tire. - Make sure that there are no stains, dust, or dirt on the lens of the multipurpose camera unit and the windshield area where the camera is mounted. - Make sure that all doors are closed. - Make sure the suspension has not been modified. - Make sure all the tire sizes are correct and that their pressure is correct. - Make sure the fuel tank is full. - Make sure the steering wheel is pointed straight ahead, and do not turn it after setting the vehicle. - Shift the shift lever to P, and apply the parking brake.

- - Aiming the multipurpose camera requires the HDS.

- Multipurpose Camera - Static Camera Aim Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Marking of the Target Position 1. Pull a string (A) between point Z1 and point Y1, and tape both ends of the string on the floor surface. NOTE: Repeat this step for the opposite side for points Z2 and Y2. 2. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 3. Set the string (A) between point B and point C, and tape both ends of the string on the floor surface. Mark new point D where the string crosses with the center-line (E). Special Tools Set-Up, Target Pattern Surface Height Adjustment, and Positioning 4. Set-up the target Install the stand pole (A) onto the stand (B). NOTE: The stand pole 1, 000 mm (39.37 in) is too low to align the multipurpose camera, and cannot be used for this procedure. You must use stand pole 07AAK-5K0A120. -2. Route the cord through the collar (C) and place it on the aiming stand. -3. Install the attachment bracket (D) onto the stand pole. -4. Assemble the target holders (E). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 5. Install the target holder assemblies onto the attachment bracket (one on each side), with the target mounting holes (A) facing toward the vehicle. NOTE: Make sure each horizontal pole of target holder are fully seated against the attachment bracket. 6. Install the targets onto the horizontal pole of target holder. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 7. Check the height (950 mm (37.40 in)) of all three targets at the center mark (B). The center target should be the same height as the outer targets. If necessary, adjust the center target by moving the target adjuster up or down on the stand pole to match the heights of the outer targets. 8. Place the aiming target assembly on mark D. NOTE: Make sure each horizontal pole of target holder are fully seated against the attachment bracket.Make sure each vertical pole (C) of target holder is on the string (A). Choosing the Setting Value of the Front Wheel Arch Height 9. Measure the right and left front wheel arch height "H1" from the floor surface to the center of the wheel arch. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 10. Calculate the average value between the left and right front wheel arch height (H1). This average is referenced as "H2". Example Left front wheel arch height : 691 mm (27.20 in) Right front wheel arch height : 690 mm (27.17 in) H2 = (691 mm (27.20 in) + 690 mm (27.17 in)) / 2 = 690.5 mm (27.185 in) 11. Subtract the front wheel arch height standard value "H3" from the average value of "H2".H = H2 - H3 Wheel Arch Height Standard Value "H3": 698 mm (27.48 in) Example H = 690.5 mm (27.185 in) - 698 mm (27.48 in) = -7.5 mm (0.295 in)12. Refer to the following table to determine the difference "H" between the mean height of the front wheel arch "H2" and the standard height "H3". Setting value number Choose the value H from following ranges (1) +5 mm (0.20 in) <= H <= +15 mm (0.59 in). (2) -5 mm (0.20 in) <= H < +5 mm (0.20 in). (3) -15 mm (0.59 in) <= H < -5 mm (0.20 in). (4) -25 mm (0.98 in) <= H < -15 mm (0.59 in).

Implementation of the Static Camera Aiming 13. Apply the parking brake. 14. Connect the HDS 15. Choosing the Setting Value of the Front Wheel Arch Height

9. Measure the right and left front wheel arch height "H1" from the floor surface to the center of the wheel arch.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

10. Calculate the average value between the left and right front wheel arch height (H1). This average is referenced as "H2".

Example

Left front wheel arch height | : 691 mm (27.20 in)

Right front wheel arch height | : 690 mm (27.17 in)

H2 = (691 mm (27.20 in) + 690 mm (27.17 in)) / 2 = 690.5 mm (27.185 in)

11. Subtract the front wheel arch height standard value "H3" from the average value of "H2".H = H2 - H3

Wheel Arch Height Standard Value "H3": | 698 mm (27.48 in)

Example H = 690.5 mm (27.185 in) - 698 mm (27.48 in) = -7.5 mm (0.295 in)12. Refer to the following table to determine the difference "H" between the mean height of the front wheel arch "H2" and the standard height "H3".

Setting value number | Choose the value H from following ranges

(1) | +5 mm (0.20 in) <= H <= +15 mm (0.59 in).

(2) | -5 mm (0.20 in) <= H < +5 mm (0.20 in).

(3) | -15 mm (0.59 in) <= H < -5 mm (0.20 in).

(4) | -25 mm (0.98 in) <= H < -15 mm (0.59 in).

(5) | -35 mm (1.38 in) <= H < -25 mm (0.98 in).

Example H = -7.5 mm (0.295 in)The setting value number which corresponds to the numerical value is (3).

NOTE:

- Use this setting value number when choosing the front wheel arch height in the HDS.

- If the measured value exceeds +15 mm (0.59 in) or less than -35 mm (1.38 in), the multipurpose camera aiming will not work. Check for frame/suspension damage or modification.

Implementation of the Static Camera Aiming

13. Apply the parking brake.

14. Connect the HDS

15. In the DRIVING SUPPORT MENU, then INTEGRATED DRIVER SUPPORT SYSTEM, then ADJUSTMENT, then select Static Camera Aiming on the HDS. After the CAUTION SCREEN, the LKAS indicator blinks and "CAM" is displayed at the MID.

NOTE: The static camera aiming status can be confirmed by the indicators on the MID.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

If the aiming has failed or is aborted, you must start the static camera aiming procedure again. 18.

- Multipurpose Camera - Dynamic Camera Aim Precaution for the Dynamic Camera Aiming: Carry out the dynamic camera aiming on a flat and straight road that has several lanes that will allow the vehicle to be driven at speeds between 45-70 mph (72-112 km/h) for 10 minutes or more. RT MENU, select INTEGRATED DRIVER SUPPORT SYSTEM, then ADJUSTMENT, then select Dynamic Camera Aiming on the HDS. "CAM" of the MID begins to blink when issuing instructions. 4. If "CAM" is displayed on the MID, the aiming is not finished. Do the dynamic camera aiming procedure again. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 7. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. 8. Disconnect the HDS.

Precaution for the Dynamic Camera Aiming:

- Carry out the dynamic camera aiming on a flat and straight road that has several lanes that will allow the vehicle to be driven at speeds between 45-70 mph (72-112 km/h) for 10 minutes or more.

- You must complete the dynamic camera aiming within 30 minutes or the aiming will fail.

- Poor weather such as rain, fog, or snow may prevent the camera from aiming.

- Wet roads can also affect the aiming procedure if the yellow or white lines are not visible because of reflections caused by the water.

- When you do the dynamic camera aiming in the dark, turn the headlights on and ensure that the windshield and headlights are clean.

- If the dynamic camera aiming fails for some reason, an ERROR with detection item is displayed on the HDS. In this case, carry out the troubleshooting for an ERROR with detection item indicated using the HDS.

- When doing the aiming procedure, make sure you drive in the center of the lane and avoid using the windshield wipers or the turn signal.

1. Start the engine.

2. Check the multipurpose camera unit DTCs with the HDS

The multipurpose camera aiming procedure can be done when this DTC is shown but if any other DTC is indicated, troubleshoot the DTC before continuing.

3. In the DRIVING SUPPORT MENU, select INTEGRATED DRIVER SUPPORT SYSTEM, then ADJUSTMENT, then select Dynamic Camera Aiming on the HDS. "CAM" of the MID begins to blink when issuing instructions.

4. Carry out the aiming procedure according to the instructions indicated on the HDS screen. If the dynamic camera aiming fails for any reason, the HDS will display an ERROR with detection item. In this case, carry out the troubleshooting for an ERROR with detection item, then retry the aiming procedure.

NOTE: Make sure that only DTC B2A60-52 indicated.

NOTE: The dynamic camera aiming status can be confirmed by the indicators on the MID.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

5. Confirm "Dynamic Camera Aiming is completed" on the HDS.

6. Stop the vehicle after confirming that the aiming is done. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, then to the ON mode, and make sure the LKAS indicator and the CAM indicator do not appear on the MID. If any DTCs are indicated, troubleshoot the indicated DTC.

NOTE: The dynamic camera aiming status can be confirmed on the MID and LKAS indicator as follows. If "CAM" is displayed on the MID, the aiming is not finished. Do the dynamic camera aiming procedure again.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

7. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

8. Disconnect the HDS.

- Aiming Failure Index NOTE: When the aiming procedure fails, the ERROR indicator is displayed on the HDS. When the Camera Aiming Fails Detection Item Diagnostic Procedure Multipurpose camera unit function failure Check the multipurpose camera unit DTCs with the HDS and do the indicated DTC troubleshooting. After the DTC has cleared, do the aiming procedure again. The position of three targets are not correct which is prescribed distance, height, or distance between the targets are not equal The target is not properly positioned. The target is not horizontally set up.
````

## Chunk 1768: Auto High-Beam (High-Beam Support System) Description - Overview (2019 2020 2021)

- Title: Auto High-Beam (High-Beam Support System) Description - Overview (2019 2020 2021)
- Source path: `pages\841.html`

- Images: `images\GHH402165.jpeg`, `images\GHH402166.jpeg`, `images\GHH402167.jpeg`

### Full Text

````text
# Auto High-Beam (High-Beam Support System) Description - Overview (2019 2020 2021)

Overview

The system operates by switching the high beam and low beam automatically depending on the road condition of vehicles running ahead and opposite vehicles, using the multipurpose camera unit.

Operating Conditions

- The combination light switch is in the AUTO position.

- The headlight low beam is turned on.

- White and red light sources are not detected ahead.

- 2 or more streetlights have not been detected.

- The vehicle speed is above 25 mph (40 km/h).

- There is no halation state in front of the vehicle.

- There is no manual operation input of the combination light switch. NOTE: Weather and road conditions (undulation, curve, or environment illuminance etc.) may affect system operation.

NOTE: Weather and road conditions (undulation, curve, or environment illuminance etc.) may affect system operation.

Stopping Condition

The system stops operation under the following conditions.

- When the combination light switch is turned to other than the AUTO position.

- When the dimmer switch is operated.

- When the vehicle speed is low:

- - Less than 6 mph (10 km/h) - Less than 15 mph (24 km/h) condition continues more than four seconds.

- - Less than 6 mph (10 km/h)

Less than 6 mph (10 km/h)

- - Less than 15 mph (24 km/h) condition continues more than four seconds.

Less than 15 mph (24 km/h) condition continues more than four seconds.

Conditions that the System Switches to Low Beam

- When there are objects that are emitting lights such as streetlights, house lights, advertising display lighting, signages, vending machines, or other reflecting light.

- When there are objects emitting lights on the road similar to the taillights of vehicles driving ahead or headlights of opposing vehicles.

- When the red lights of the traffic signals are detected.

- When the front wiper motor operates at HIGH for 5 seconds.

Conditions that Switching Timing to Low Beam is Changed

- When the taillights of the vehicle driving ahead are bright or dark.

- When the headlights of the opposite vehicle are bright or dark.

- In rainy, snowy, and foggy weather.

Detection Range and Limits

The multipurpose camera unit recognizes and reacts within the range of 25 degrees horizontally (total 50 degrees).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Auto High-Beam Operation Indicator

When a system malfunction occurs, or when the camera lens or the windshield gets dirty, the warning message is shown on the multi-information display (MID). If the warning message is shown, the system stops operating.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

NOTE: The actual message may be different depending on your market.
````
