## Chunk 1771: Driving Support System Description - Overview (2019 2020 2021)

- Title: Driving Support System Description - Overview (2019 2020 2021)
- Source path: `pages\844.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_1073e89cd67a`
- Images: `images\GHH402170.jpeg`, `images\GHH402171.jpeg`, `images\GHH402172.jpeg`, `images\GHH402173.jpeg`, `images\GHH402174.jpeg`, `images\GHH402175.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1326.html`, `pages\26133.html`, `pages\16400.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Driving Support System Description - Overview (2019 2020 2021)

Integrated Driver Support System

This vehicle is equipped with the integrated driver support system, which analyzes information from the monocular camera (multipurpose camera) and millimeter wave radar in a comprehensive manner to recognize an object.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Roles of Each Function

The integrated Driver Support System has three main categories of functions: Safety, Comfort and Lighting control functions. When the integrated driver support system detects a hazard, an alarm and/or warning notification is triggered. The system provides safety functions that perform a hazard evasion assistance if the driver does not (or cannot) respond to the warning notification or alarm. The system also provides comfort and lighting control functions controlling the vehicle behavior according to the road conditions to assist the driver.

Safety Functions | Driving Assist Functions | Lighting Control Function

CMBS, Road Departure Mitigation (RDM) | ACC with Low Speed Follow, LKAS | Auto High-Beam

The function is ON all the time (can be turned off by switch operation) | The function is selectable by the user | The function is ON by turning the combination light switch to the AUTO position

Operational priority of each function

Operation priority of each function is set among systems. The CMBS gets activated when a risk of collision becomes greater while the ACC control is in operation. Once the CMBS becomes active, the ACC will be cancelled. When the RDM System is activated, its operation has higher priority than LKAS. Between CMBS and RDM System, the brake control of the CMBS has higher priority.

Operation of the Millimeter Wave Radar

The millimeter wave radar uses millimeter wave bands to detect vehicles and pedestrians (vehicle ahead, opposite vehicles and pedestrian) in front of the vehicle. The radar also calculates the relative speed of the objects and the distance between the objects and the vehicle by transmitting and receiving the millimeter waves.

Operation Conditions

Item | Leading Vehicle

Target Detection Range | 0.5 - 120 m (1.6 - 394 ft)

Millimeter waves are emitted within an angle range of 40 degrees in the vertical direction and an angle of 90 degrees in the horizontal direction.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Vehicle/Object Detection Process

The multipurpose camera unit determines if there is any leading vehicle and/or detected objects that are moving based on the outlines of the objects, relative position and speed of the objects. In order to distinguish between leading vehicles and pedestrians, the system uses the recognition result received from both the millimeter wave radar and the multipurpose camera as follows.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Pedestrian Image Processing

The multipurpose camera is able to detect pedestrians. When the system detects an object that could be a pedestrian, the system compares the object's characteristics against programmed characteristics for the system to determine if the object is likely a pedestrian or other object.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Function Failure Indication

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

# Driving Support System Description - Overview (2/4-door) (2016 2017 2018 2019)

Integrated Driver Support System

This vehicle is equipped with the Integrated Driver Support System, which integrates and analyzes the information transmitted from the millimeter wave radar and the multipurpose camera to recognize objects.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

*: Depending on the vehicle trim level, some vehicles may use a millimeter wave radar and a multipurpose camera while other trims may only use a multipurpose camera.

Roles of Each Function

The Integrated Driver Support System has two main categories of functions: Safety and Comfort functions. When the Integrated Driver Support System detects a hazard, an alarm and/or warning notification is triggered. The system provides safety functions that perform a hazard evasion assistance if the driver does not (or/cannot) respond to the warning notification or alarm. The system also provides comfort functions controlling the vehicle behavior according to the road conditions to assist the driver.

Safety Functions | Comfort Functions

CMBS, Road Departure Mitigation (RDM) | ACC with Low Speed Follow (LSF), LKAS

The function is basically ON all the time (can be turned off by switch operation) | The function is selectable by the users

Operational priority of each function

Operation priority of each function is set among systems. The CMBS gets activated when a risk of collision becomes greater while the ACC control is in operation. Once the CMBS becomes active, the ACC will be cancelled. When the RDM System is activated, its operation has higher priority than LKAS. Between CMBS and RDM System, the brake control of the CMBS has higher priority.

Operation of the Millimeter Wave Radar

The millimeter wave radar uses millimeter wave bands to detect vehicles and pedestrians (leading and opposite vehicles) in front of the vehicle. The radar also calculates the relative speed of the objects and the distance between the objects and the vehicle by transmitting and receiving the millimeter waves.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Operation Conditions

Item | Leading Vehicle | Opposite vehicle | Pedestrian output

Target Detection Range | 0.5 - 120 m (1.6 - 394 ft) | 1 - 120 m (3 - 394 ft) | 1 - 40 m (3 - 131 ft)

Vehicle/Object Detection Process

The multipurpose camera unit determines if there is any leading vehicle and/or detected objects that are moving based on the outlines of the objects, relative position and speed of the objects. In order to distinguish between leading vehicles and pedestrians, the system uses the recognition result received from both the millimeter wave radar and the multipurpose camera as follows.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Pedestrian Image Processing

The multipurpose camera is able to detect pedestrians. When the system detects an object that could be a pedestrian, the system compares the object's characteristics against programmed characteristics for the system to determine if the object is likely a pedestrian or other object.

When the vehicle is also equipped with a millimeter wave radar, the system uses both the radar information and the multipurpose camera information to determine if the object is likely a pedestrian.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Function Failure Indication

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

## Chunk 1773: Driving Support System Description - Overview (5-door) (2017 2018 2019)

- Title: Driving Support System Description - Overview (5-door) (2017 2018 2019)
- Source path: `pages\846.html`

- Images: `images\GHH402182.jpeg`, `images\GHH402183.jpeg`, `images\GHH402184.jpeg`, `images\GHH402185.jpeg`, `images\GHH402186.jpeg`, `images\GHH402187.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1328.html`, `pages\26135.html`, `pages\16402.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Driving Support System Description - Overview (5-door) (2017 2018 2019)

Integrated Driver Support System

This vehicle is equipped with the integrated driver support system, which analyzes information from the monocular camera (multipurpose camera) and millimeter wave radar in a comprehensive manner to recognize an object.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Roles of Each Function

The integrated Driver Support System has three main categories of functions: Safety, Comfort and Lighting control functions. When the integrated driver support system detects a hazard, an alarm and/or warning notification is triggered. The system provides safety functions that perform a hazard evasion assistance if the driver does not (or cannot) respond to the warning notification or alarm. The system also provides comfort and lighting control functions controlling the vehicle behavior according to the road conditions to assist the driver.

Safety Functions | Driving Assist Functions | Lighting Control Function

CMBS, Road Departure Mitigation (RDM) | ACC with Low Speed Follow, LKAS | Auto High-Beam

The function is ON all the time (can be turned off by switch operation) | The function is selectable by the user | The function is ON by turning the combination light switch to the AUTO position

Operational priority of each function

Operation priority of each function is set among systems. The CMBS gets activated when a risk of collision becomes greater while the ACC control is in operation. Once the CMBS becomes active, the ACC will be cancelled. When the RDM System is activated, its operation has higher priority than LKAS. Between CMBS and RDM System, the brake control of the CMBS has higher priority.

Operation of the Millimeter Wave Radar

The millimeter wave radar uses millimeter wave bands to detect vehicles and pedestrians (vehicle ahead, opposite vehicles and pedestrian) in front of the vehicle. The radar also calculates the relative speed of the objects and the distance between the objects and the vehicle by transmitting and receiving the millimeter waves.

Operation Conditions

Item | Leading Vehicle

Target Detection Range | 0.5 - 120 m (1.6 - 394 ft)

Millimeter waves are emitted within an angle range of 40 degrees in the vertical direction and an angle of 90 degrees in the horizontal direction.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Vehicle/Object Detection Process

The multipurpose camera unit determines if there is any leading vehicle and/or detected objects that are moving based on the outlines of the objects, relative position and speed of the objects. In order to distinguish between leading vehicles and pedestrians, the system uses the recognition result received from both the millimeter wave radar and the multipurpose camera as follows.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Pedestrian Image Processing

The multipurpose camera is able to detect pedestrians. When the system detects an object that could be a pedestrian, the system compares the object's characteristics against programmed characteristics for the system to determine if the object is likely a pedestrian or other object.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Function Failure Indication

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

## Chunk 1774: Road Departure Mitigation System Description - Overview

- Title: Road Departure Mitigation System Description - Overview
- Source path: `pages\847.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_e0b90fd90489`
- Images: `images\GHH402188.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1329.html`, `pages\26136.html`, `pages\16403.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Road Departure Mitigation System Description - Overview

The Road Departure Mitigation (RDM) System uses image information from the multipurpose camera to recognize the lane markings on the road. The multipurpose camera unit monitors the lane markings and detects risks of the vehicle moving out of its lane. When the unit determines that the vehicle begins to move out of its lane, the unit warns the driver by vibrating the steering wheel, showing a road-departure warning image on the multi information display (MID) and also provides a steering control to assist steering the vehicle back in its lane to prevent the vehicle from moving out of its lane. When the multipurpose camera unit determines that a steering control is not sufficient to prevent the vehicle moving out if its lane and the approaching lane marking on the road is indicated as a solid line, the unit also provides a brake control to assist decelerating the vehicle. When the vehicle goes out of a lane or the multipurpose camera unit determines that the vehicle begins to move out of its lanes for the third time after moving out of its lane twice in a row without the driver's steering action, the unit warns the driver with only an MID warning image and a buzzer sound without providing steering control.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Operation Conditions

System Operation Conditions | The Road Departure Mitigation (RDM) System works as long as an approaching lane marking is recognizable (lane markings may not be recognizable under some conditions such as bad weather, backlit, and on snowy roads).

Operation vehicle speed | 45 - 90 mph (72 - 145 km/h)

Object lane line types | Broken lines on the road | The road-departure warning image on the multi information display (MID) and steering wheel assist control.

Solid lines on the road | The road-departure warning image on the multi information display (MID), steering wheel assist control and brake control to assist decelerating the vehicle.

Driver operation | The vehicle will not react if the system receives inputs to indicate that the driver is intentionally departed the road by movements of the steering wheel, accelerator pedal, brake pedal, turn signal switch, etc.
````

## Chunk 1794: Auto High-Beam Indicator (Green)

- Title: Auto High-Beam Indicator (Green)
- Source path: `pages\866.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Auto High-Beam Indicator (Green)

When the Auto High-Beam is OK, the Auto High-Beam indicates its operation on the gauge control module as below.

NOTE:

- For Auto High-Beam function, review the Auto High-Beam Description .

- The Auto High-Beam communication with other control units via the F-CAN. For Auto High-Beam diagram, review the Auto High-Beam Diagram . If a connected unit malfunctions, the Auto High-Beam will not operate. In this case, both Auto High-Beam indicator and connected unit indicator come on and stay on (except for the gauge control module).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

## Chunk 1795: Self-Diagnostic Function

- Title: Self-Diagnostic Function
- Source path: `pages\867.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_00c47c4b6257`
- Images: `images\GHH402227.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1349.html`, `pages\26156.html`, `pages\16419.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Self-Diagnostic Function

The Auto High-Beam has a self-diagnostic function. If a malfunction of the Auto High-Beam detects a problem while in operation, a warning message is displayed on the multi-information display (MID), the Auto High-Beam stops operating, and stores DTCs.

NOTE: The Auto High-Beam operates while maintaining a mutual connection between the multipurpose camera unit at the control origin and the other control units (relay control module, MICU, gauge control module, automatic lighting/rain sensor, etc.). If malfunctions occur in these control unit systems, the Auto High-Beam does not operate. At this time, warning indicator of the related control unit turn on and a warning message is displayed on the multi-information display (MID).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

## Chunk 1796: Dirt or dust detective function (CLEAN FRONT WINDSHIELD Indication)

- Title: Dirt or dust detective function (CLEAN FRONT WINDSHIELD Indication)
- Source path: `pages\868.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_513de9772786`
- Images: `images\GHH402228.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1350.html`, `pages\26157.html`, `pages\16420.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Dirt or dust detective function (CLEAN FRONT WINDSHIELD Indication)

If the message Some Driver Assist System Cannot Operate: Clean Front Windshield appears on the MID, the camera lens or the windshield has enough dirt so that the camera view is obstructed. The Auto High-Beam stops operating.

Clean any dirt or contamination on the camera lens or the windshield. Once the camera's view is unobstructed, the MID turns off and the Auto High-Beam automatically turns back to ON.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

## Chunk 1797: Multipurpose Camera unit

- Title: Multipurpose Camera unit
- Source path: `pages\869.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Multipurpose Camera unit

The Auto High-Beam of this vehicle is controlled by the multipurpose camera unit. If an error or malfunction of the multipurpose camera unit occurs, the Auto High-Beam does not operate. At this time, a warning message is displayed on the multi-information display (MID). The operations of the Auto High-Beam corresponding to the multipurpose camera unit malfunctions can be checked in the troubleshooting list by each DTC for the multipurpose camera unit .
````

## Chunk 1798: System Protect Function (High temperature) (MULTIPURPOSE CAMERA HOT Indication)

- Title: System Protect Function (High temperature) (MULTIPURPOSE CAMERA HOT Indication)
- Source path: `pages\870.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_7f92730c97df`
- Images: `images\GHH402229.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1352.html`, `pages\26159.html`, `pages\16422.html`

### Full Text

````text
# System Protect Function (High temperature) (MULTIPURPOSE CAMERA HOT Indication)

If the message Some Driver Assist Systems Cannot Operate: Camera Temperature Too High appears on the MID, the Auto High-Beam stops operating, and stores a DTC.

When the multipurpose camera temperature cools down to its proper operating temperature, the indicator on the MID turns off, and the Auto High-Beam automatically resumes operation.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

# CAN communication

The Auto High-Beam communicates with other control units via the F-CAN. For Auto High-Beam diagram, review the Auto High-Beam Diagram .

If the multipurpose camera unit detects a communication error or a malfunction in another connected unit, the Auto High-Beam will not operate even though it is OK. In this case, both the Auto High-Beam indicator and the related unit indicator will come on and stay on.
````

## Chunk 1801: Intermittent Failures

- Title: Intermittent Failures
- Source path: `pages\873.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Intermittent Failures

The term "intermittent failure" means a system may have had a failure, but it checks OK now. If you cannot reproduce the condition, check for intermittent failure DTCs. If intermittent failure DTCs are not stored, check for loose connections and terminals. Also check ground and power connections related to the circuit that you are troubleshooting.
````

## Chunk 1802: How to Troubleshoot DTCs

- Title: How to Troubleshoot DTCs
- Source path: `pages\874.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_bf3b82ad3aab`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1356.html`, `pages\26163.html`, `pages\16426.html`

### Full Text

````text
# How to Troubleshoot DTCs

Check the DTCs with the HDS.

NOTE: For Auto High-Beam DTCs, refer to the DTC troubleshooting index .

Before troubleshooting, check and note these items:

- Ask the customer about the conditions when the problem occurred, and try to reproduce the same conditions for troubleshooting.

- If the symptom does not appear and the Auto High-Beam indicator does not come on while test-driving, but your troubleshooting was based on the DTC, check for poor connections or loose terminals at all connectors related to the circuit that you are troubleshooting.

- After the troubleshooting or repairs are done, clear the DTCs, and test-drive the vehicle under the same conditions as when the DTC was originally set. Make sure the Auto High-Beam warning message does not come on.

- The Auto High-Beam sometimes stops operating because of the environment (weather, road conditions, driving conditions, etc.). A intermittent failure DTCs.
````

## Chunk 1803: How to Retrieve DTCs

- Title: How to Retrieve DTCs
- Source path: `pages\875.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_fee12833175e`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1357.html`, `pages\26164.html`, `pages\16427.html`

### Full Text

````text
# How to Retrieve DTCs

NOTE:

- Make sure the 12 volt battery is fully charged before you begin.

- The Auto High-Beam of this vehicle is controlled by the multipurpose camera unit. For this reason, the Auto High-Beam may not operate normally depending on the malfunction status of the multipurpose camera unit. The operations of the Auto High-Beam related to the multipurpose camera unit malfunctions can be checked in the troubleshooting list by DTC for the millimeter wave radar .

1. Connect the HDS

2. Use the HDS to check for Integrated Driver Support System for DTCs.

3. Read and record the DTC: 4. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

5. Disconnect the HDS.

6. Do the troubleshooting procedure for the DTC.
````

## Chunk 1804: How to Clear DTCs

- Title: How to Clear DTCs
- Source path: `pages\876.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_958c30259492`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1108.html`, `pages\1358.html`, `pages\26165.html`, `pages\16694.html`, `pages\16428.html`

### Full Text

````text
# How to Clear DTCs

NOTE: Make sure the 12 volt battery is fully charged before you begin.

1. Connect the HDS

2. In the DRIVING SUPPORT MENU of the HDS, select Integrated Driver Support System, then choose the clear DTCs icon, and follow the prompts.

3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

4. Disconnect the HDS.
````

# RDM Indicator (Amber)

When the RDM system detects a problem during operation, the RDM system stops operating, and the RDM indicator and the brake system indicator (amber) turns on.

NOTE:

- For RDM system function, review the Road Departure Mitigation (RDM) Description .

- The RDM system communicates with other control units via the F-CAN. For RDM system diagram, review the RDM System Diagram . If a connected unit malfunctions, the RDM system will not operate. In this case, both RDM indicator and connected unit indicator come on and stay on (except for the gauge control module).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

## Chunk 1808: Millimeter Wave Radar

- Title: Millimeter Wave Radar
- Source path: `pages\880.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_6a374a3f6cf6`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1362.html`, `pages\26169.html`, `pages\16432.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Millimeter Wave Radar

The millimeter wave radar measures the distance between your vehicle and the vehicle directly ahead to calculate the difference in speed between the vehicles.

2/4-door: The millimeter wave radar also has an error detection function which sends a signal to the multipurpose camera unit if an error is detected.

5-door: The millimeter wave radar has a self-diagnostic function. If the system detects a problem, it disables the RDM system, and turns on the RDM indicator.
````

## Chunk 1809: Dirt or dust detective function (Clean Millimeter Wave Radar Indication)

- Title: Dirt or dust detective function (Clean Millimeter Wave Radar Indication)
- Source path: `pages\881.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_d48283298669`
- Images: `images\GHH402232.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1363.html`, `pages\26170.html`, `pages\16433.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Dirt or dust detective function (Clean Millimeter Wave Radar Indication)

If the millimeter wave radar or radar cover gets dirty, the RDM system stops operating and the following message will appear: Some Driver Assist Systems Cannot Operate: Radar Obstructed appears on the MID. Clean the millimeter wave radar and the radar cover surface. Once the millimeter wave radar and radar cover surface are clean, the message on the MID disappears and the RDM system automatically turns on.

NOTE:

- Under the above conditions DTC P2583-97 is stored.

- When it is snowing, the radio waves transmitted and received by the millimeter wave radar may be refracted by the snow or ice attached around the millimeter wave radar such as the front grille. At this time, DTC P2583-76 is stored depending on the situation.

- After DTC P2583-76 is stored, the message (Radar Obstructed) is displayed on the MID at the initial stage in some models, and then the indicators come on.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

## Chunk 1811: Multipurpose Camera Unit

### Full Text

````text
# Multipurpose Camera Unit

The multipurpose camera unit uses input from the EPS motor/control unit, the SRS unit, the PCM, and the VSA modulator-control unit to warn the driver when approaching the vehicle ahead. The multipurpose camera unit communicates with the gauge control module to send the warning signal.

The multipurpose camera unit also has an error detection function which sends a signal to the millimeter wave radar if an error is detected.
````

## Chunk 1813: System Protect Function (High temperature) (MULTIPURPOSE CAMERA HOT Indication)

- Title: System Protect Function (High temperature) (MULTIPURPOSE CAMERA HOT Indication)
- Source path: `pages\885.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_0d3acff76121`
- Images: `images\GHH402234.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1367.html`, `pages\26174.html`, `pages\16437.html`

### Full Text

````text
# System Protect Function (High temperature) (MULTIPURPOSE CAMERA HOT Indication)

If the message Some Driver Assist Systems Cannot Operate: Camera Temperature Too High appears on the MID, the RDM system stops operating.

2/4-door: When the multipurpose camera temperature cools down to its proper operating temperature, the indicator on the MID turns off, and the RDM system automatically resumes operation.

5-door: When the multipurpose camera temperature cools down to its proper operating temperature, the indicator on the MID turns off, and the RDM system automatically resumes operation, and stores a DTC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

````text
# Multipurpose Camera Aiming Function

If the multipurpose camera unit is removed or replaced, you must do the multipurpose camera aiming procedure. If the aiming is incomplete, the RDM indicator blinks.
````

## Chunk 1815: CAN Communications

- Title: CAN Communications
- Source path: `pages\887.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_6394013ad541`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1369.html`, `pages\26176.html`, `pages\16439.html`

### Full Text

````text
# CAN Communications

The RDM system communicates with other control units via the F-CAN. For RDM system diagram, review the RDM System Diagram .

If the multipurpose camera unit detects a communication error or a malfunction in another connected unit, the RDM system will not operate even though it is OK. In this case, both RDM indicator and related unit indicator will come on and stay on.
````

## Chunk 1816: How to Troubleshoot DTCs

- Title: How to Troubleshoot DTCs
- Source path: `pages\889.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_3b25839d6548`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1371.html`, `pages\26178.html`, `pages\16441.html`

### Full Text

````text
# How to Troubleshoot DTCs

Check the DTCs with the HDS.

NOTE: For RDM system DTCs, refer to the DTC troubleshooting index .

Before troubleshooting, check and note these items:

- Ask the customer about the conditions when the problem occurred, and try to reproduce the same conditions for troubleshooting.

- If the symptom does not appear and the RDM indicator does not come on while test-driving, but your troubleshooting was based on the DTC, check for poor connections or loose terminals at all connectors related to the circuit that you are troubleshooting.

- After the troubleshooting or repairs are done, clear the DTCs, and test-drive the vehicle under the same conditions as when the DTC was originally set. Make sure the RDM indicator does not come on.

- The RDM system sometimes stops operating because of the environment (weather, road conditions, driving conditions, etc.). A intermittent failure DTCs.
````

## Chunk 1817: How to Retrieve DTCs

- Title: How to Retrieve DTCs
- Source path: `pages\890.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_42a71927b3ef`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1372.html`, `pages\26179.html`, `pages\16442.html`

### Full Text

````text
# How to Retrieve DTCs

NOTE: Make sure the 12 volt battery is fully charged before you begin.

1. Connect the HDS

2. Use the HDS to check for Integrated Driver Support System for DTCs.

3. Read and record the DTC.

4. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, then wait for 10 seconds.

5. Disconnect the HDS.

6. Do the troubleshooting procedure for the DTC.
````

## Chunk 1818: How to Clear DTCs

- Title: How to Clear DTCs
- Source path: `pages\891.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_8a5610b08b8b`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1373.html`, `pages\26180.html`, `pages\16443.html`

### Full Text

````text
# How to Clear DTCs

NOTE: Make sure the 12 volt battery is fully charged before you begin.

1. Connect the HDS

2. In the DRIVING SUPPORT MENU of the HDS, select Integrated Driver Support System, then choose the clear DTCs icon, and follow the prompts.

3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, then wait for 10 seconds.

4. Disconnect the HDS.
````

# Millimeter Wave Radar Mounting Position Inspection (2/4-door) (2019 2020 2021): Inspection

NOTE: If millimeter wave radar aiming cannot be completed, follow the procedure below to check that the millimeter wave radar is installed properly and the radar bracket is not deformed. Based on the check results, replace faulty parts. After that, perform millimeter wave radar aiming again.

- Front Bumper - Remove

- Millimeter Wave Radar Mounting Position - Inspect Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. NOTE: When the millimeter wave radar is adjusted on a floor surface that has more than one degree of horizontal slope (drainage slope), the vertical radar aiming cannot be adjusted correctly. Check the levelness of the floor surface, but if there is a continuous slope, then adjust the angle of millimeter wave radar so it is parallel to the floor surface. Lay a ruler or other flat straight object on the floor surface and put the level on top to confirm the levelness of the floor surface. Make sure the steering wheel is pointed straight ahead, and do not turn it after setting up the vehicle. 1. Put the level on the millimeter wave radar. NOTE: Use the level included in the alignment set. To correctly confirm the bubble position in the level, have a look from the side. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. Confirm if the bubble in the level is between the lines. NOTE: If the bubble is not within the lines, adjust the mounting position using the vertical adjustment bolt (A). 3. Remove the level.

# Millimeter Wave Radar Mounting Position Inspection (4-door, Japan Production) (2018): Inspection

NOTE:

- If millimeter wave radar aiming cannot be completed, follow the procedure below to check that the millimeter wave radar is installed properly and the radar bracket is not deformed.

- Based on the check results, replace faulty parts. After that, perform millimeter wave radar aiming again.

- Radar Cover - Remove

- Millimeter Wave Radar Mounting Position - Inspect Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. NOTE: Make sure the steering wheel is pointed straight ahead, and do not turn it after setting up the vehicle. 1. Put the level on the aiming pad of the millimeter wave radar. NOTE: Use the level included in the alignment set. 2. Confirm if the bubble in the level is between the lines. Check visually using a mirror (A). NOTE: To correctly confirm the bubble position in the level, hold the mirror above the level so that the level in the mirror is seen as a rectangle. If the bubble is not within the lines, adjust the mounting position using the vertical adjustment bolt (B). If you cannot adjust the millimeter wave radar horizontally, check for damage on the millimeter wave radar bracket or the vehicle frame. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | NOTE: Make sure the steering wheel is pointed straight ahead, and do not turn it after setting up the vehicle. 1. Put the level on the aiming pad of the millimeter wave radar. NOTE: Use the level included in the alignment set.

1. Put the level on the aiming pad of the millimeter wave radar.

NOTE: Use the level included in the alignment set.

2. Confirm if the bubble in the level is between the lines. Check visually using a mirror (A).

- To correctly confirm the bubble position in the level, hold the mirror above the level so that the level in the mirror is seen as a rectangle.

- If the bubble is not within the lines, adjust the mounting position using the vertical adjustment bolt (B).

- If you cannot adjust the millimeter wave radar horizontally, check for damage on the millimeter wave radar bracket or the vehicle frame.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

- Millimeter Wave Radar - Aim

# Road Departure Mitigation Symptom Troubleshooting - RDM indicator (on the MID) does not come on

- Gauge control module operation check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. Check the following indicators in the gauge control module: Malfunction indicator lamp (MIL) Brake system indicator Battery charging system indicator Do the indicators come on? YES Go to the gauge control module self-diagnostic function . NO Go to step 2.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

Check the following indicators in the gauge control module:

- Malfunction indicator lamp (MIL)

- Brake system indicator

- Battery charging system indicator

Do the indicators come on?

YES

Go to the gauge control module self-diagnostic function .

NO

Go to step 2.

- Fuse check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Check the following fuse. Fuse No. B7 (10 A) Location Under-dash fuse/relay box Is the fuse OK? YES The fuse is OK. Reinstall the fuse, then go to step 3. NO Replace the fuse. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, then the OFF (LOCK) mode. If the fuse blows again, repair the short to ground on the No. B7 (10 A) fuse circuit.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Check the following fuse.

Fuse | No. B7 (10 A)

Location | Under-dash fuse/relay box

Is the fuse OK?

YES

The fuse is OK. Reinstall the fuse, then go to step 3.

NO

Replace the fuse. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, then the OFF (LOCK) mode. If the fuse blows again, repair the short to ground on the No. B7 (10 A) fuse circuit.

- Open wire check (IG1 METER line) -1. Disconnect the following connector. Gauge control module connector A (32P) -2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -3. Measure the voltage between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle ON mode Gauge control module connector A (32P): disconnected Test point 1 Gauge control module connector A (32P) No. 17 Test point 2 Body ground Is there battery voltage? YES Replace the gauge control module . NO Repair an open in the IG1 METER wire between the under-dash fuse/relay box and the gauge control module.

-1. Disconnect the following connector.

Gauge control module connector A (32P)

-2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-3. Measure the voltage between test points 1 and 2.

Test condition | Vehicle ON mode

Gauge control module connector A (32P): disconnected

Test point 1 | Gauge control module connector A (32P) No. 17

Test point 2 | Body ground

Is there battery voltage?

YES

Replace the gauge control module .

NO

Repair an open in the IG1 METER wire between the under-dash fuse/relay box and the gauge control module.
````

# LaneWatch Camera Aiming: Adjustment

NOTE:

- The LaneWatch camera must be aimed after one or more of the following procedures are done:

- - LaneWatch camera removal or replacement - Passenger's side power mirror removal or replacement - Passenger's side mirror housing lower cover removal or replacement - Passenger's side door position adjustment

- - LaneWatch camera removal or replacement

LaneWatch camera removal or replacement

- - Passenger's side power mirror removal or replacement

Passenger's side power mirror removal or replacement

- - Passenger's side mirror housing lower cover removal or replacement

Passenger's side mirror housing lower cover removal or replacement

- - Passenger's side door position adjustment

Passenger's side door position adjustment

- Set up the vehicle:

- - Install an aiming target on the passenger's side of the vehicle. - Do the aiming in a proper place where you can obtain the about 4.5 m (14.8 ft) distance between a target pattern and the vehicle (from the center of the front wheel hub).

- - Install an aiming target on the passenger's side of the vehicle.

Install an aiming target on the passenger's side of the vehicle.

- - Do the aiming in a proper place where you can obtain the about 4.5 m (14.8 ft) distance between a target pattern and the vehicle (from the center of the front wheel hub).

Do the aiming in a proper place where you can obtain the about 4.5 m (14.8 ft) distance between a target pattern and the vehicle (from the center of the front wheel hub).

- Prepare these items:

- - Tape measure and ruler - Felt-tip pen - Tape - String

- - Tape measure and ruler

Tape measure and ruler

- - Felt-tip pen

Felt-tip pen

- - Tape

Tape

- - String

String

- Park the vehicle on floor surface in a width open area to avoid any reflection from objects that could affect the alignment procedure. Make sure you have at least the following amount of space around the vehicle: 6.5 m (21.3 ft) in length, 3.5 m (11.5 ft) in width.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

- When marking on the floor surface, use suitable tape to mark the exact locations.

- Before Aiming - Set

- Aiming Marker - Set Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. NOTE: Make sure there are no other objects except the aiming target in this area. Check the tire pressures, and adjust them to the specified value if needed. Have the driver sit in the driver's seat, or put an object equivalent to the driver's weight in the driver's seat. 1. Set the aiming marker (A) to the LaneWatch aiming stand (B), and adjust the height of the aiming marker. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. Pull a string (A) between point Z1 and point Y1, and tape both ends of the string on the floor surface. NOTE: Repeat this step for the opposite wheels, and pull a string on the floor surface. 3. Measure and make new spot B on the floor surface in rear of the vehicle 4, 500 mm (177.17 in) from the point Z1. NOTE: Repeat this step for the opposite front wheel, and mark new spot C on the floor surface. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 4. Stretch a string (A) between point B and point C, and tape both ends of the string on the floor surface. Mark new point D where the string crosses with the center-line (E). 5. On the string (A), mark new point F (target of the passenger's side) which is 1, 300 mm (51.18 in) from point D to the passenger's side. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 6. Place the left side edge of the aiming marker (A) on the mark that you made in the previous step. Align the legs of the marker with the tape measure to make sure the marker is square to the vehicle.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | NOTE: Make sure there are no other objects except the aiming target in this area. Check the tire pressures, and adjust them to the specified value if needed. Set the aiming marker (A) to the LaneWatch aiming stand (B), and adjust the height of the aiming marker.

- Make sure there are no other objects except the aiming target in this area.

- Check the tire pressures, and adjust them to the specified value if needed.

- Have the driver sit in the driver's seat, or put an object equivalent to the driver's weight in the driver's seat.

# LaneWatch Symptom Troubleshooting - Does not switch to the LaneWatch screen

- LaneWatch system check (navigation system diagnostic mode) -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Do the navigation system diagnostic mode, and select the Detail Information and Setting . -3. Is Connection OK?

YES

Go to step 2.

NO

Check for hard error codes (navigation DTCs) .

- LaneWatch switch check (navigation system diagnostic mode) -1. Select the Functional Setup icon. -2. Select the LaneWatch icon, then operate the LaneWatch switch. Is the LaneWatch Switch OK? YES Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. NO Go to step 3.

-1. Select the Functional Setup icon.

-2. Select the LaneWatch icon, then operate the LaneWatch switch.

Is the LaneWatch Switch OK?

YES

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time.

NO

Go to step 3.

- Open wire check (LANEWATCH SW line) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the following connector. Combination light switch 12P connector -3. Connect terminals A and B with a jumper wire. Terminal A Combination light switch 12P connector No. 4 Terminal B Body ground Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. -4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -5. Do the navigation system diagnostic mode, and select the Detail Information and Setting . -6. Select the Functional Setup icon. -7. Select the LaneWatch icon, then operate the LaneWatch switch. Is the LaneWatch Switch OK? YES Go to step 4. NO Repair an open in the LANEWATCH SW wire between the combination light switch and the audio-navigation unit.

YES

Go to step 4.

NO

- Open wire check (GND line) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Remove the jumper wire. -3. Connect terminals A and B with a jumper wire. Do the navigation system diagnostic mode, and select the Detail Information and Setting . -6. Select the Functional Setup icon. -7. Select the LaneWatch icon, then operate the LaneWatch switch. ## Chunk 1860: LaneWatch System Description - Overview

- Title: LaneWatch System Description - Overview
- Source path: `pages\928.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_9fe4630efb9f`
- Images: `images\GHH411921.jpeg`, `images\GHH411922.jpeg`, `images\GHH411923.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1410.html`, `pages\26103.html`, `pages\13295.html`

### Full Text

````text
# LaneWatch System Description - Overview

LaneWatch System

LaneWatch TM uses a camera and the display to help drivers recognize objects in the passenger's side blind spot. The added visibility improves safety when changing lanes or making right turns.

The driver can see other vehicles, bicycles, or pedestrians that are not normally visible in the passenger's side mirror.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Reference line

The LaneWatch image is provided with the three reference lines, each showing the distance from the rear bumper. Three lines that appear on the display can give an idea of how far the vehicles or objects on the display are from your vehicle, respectively. If an object is near line 1 (in red), it indicates that it is close to your vehicle, whereas an object near line 3 is farther away.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Display image (Normal mode)

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

LaneWatch Camera

The LaneWatch camera is built into the passenger's side power mirror which sends the image to the display.

LaneWatch Switch

The LaneWatch switch is built into the combination light switch.

Center Display Unit

The image appears on the display.

Operation

- Whether the display on or off, pressing the LaneWatch switch will change the display to the LaneWatch screen.

- When the turn signal switch is moved to the right turn position (lane change or continuous flash), the display will change to the LaneWatch camera view.

Customization

- The turn signal switch interconnection can be switched ON or OFF.

- The LaneWatch screen reference lines can be turned ON or OFF.

- On models with navigation, route guidance screen interruption can be turned ON or OFF.

- Display settings such as brightness, contrast, black level, color and tint can be adjusted.
````

## Chunk 1861: LaneWatch System Description - System Diagram

- Title: LaneWatch System Description - System Diagram
- Source path: `pages\929.html`

### Full Text

````text
# LaneWatch System Description - System Diagram

LaneWatch Camera

LaneWatch may reduce the passenger's side blind spot by displaying the LaneWatch camera image mounted under the passenger's side power mirror. The image is displayed on the center display unit when the turn signal is activated to the passenger's side or the LaneWatch switch is pressed.

The LaneWatch obtains information from the external sensors and the switches that are connected to their respective control units.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The system diagram of the LaneWatch system is shown below.

For locations of each component on vehicle, refer to Component Location Index:

- Audio unit

- Audio-navigation unit, Center display unit

- LaneWatch camera, LaneWatch switch

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

# DTC B261C, B261D, B261E, B261F

DTC B261C : Rear 1st Sensor Data Failure

DTC B261D : Rear 2nd Sensor Data Failure

DTC B261E : Rear 3rd Sensor Data Failure

DTC B261F : Rear 4th Sensor Data Failure

NOTE:

- If you are troubleshooting multiple DTCs, be sure to follow the instructions in B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A .

- Before you troubleshoot, resetting the parking and back-up sensor control unit .

- The connection order of the sensor is 1st -> 2nd -> 3rd -> 4th from the side near the control unit.

DTC Description | DTC

B261C Rear 1st Sensor Data Failure

B261D Rear 2nd Sensor Data Failure

B261E Rear 3rd Sensor Data Failure

B261F Rear 4th Sensor Data Failure

DTC (Parking and Back-Up Sensor Control Unit)

- Problem verification -1. Clear the DTCs with the HDS. -2. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode and then the ON mode. -3. Wait for at least 6 seconds. -4. Check for DTCs with the HDS. YES Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for loose or poor connections.

DTC B2647 : Rear Sensor Power Failure

- The connection order of the sensor is 1st -> 2nd -> 3rd -> 4th from the side near the control unit.

B2647 Rear Sensor Power Failure

DTC (Parking and Back-Up Sensor Control Unit)

# DTC B2648

DTC B2648 : Front Sensor Signal Short to Ground

NOTE:

- If you are troubleshooting multiple DTCs, be sure to follow the instructions in B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A .

- The connection order of the sensor is 1st -> 2nd -> 3rd -> 4th from the side near the control unit.

DTC Description | DTC

B2648 Front Sensor Signal Short to Ground

DTC (Parking and Back-Up Sensor Control Unit)

- Problem verification -1. Clear the DTCs with the HDS. -2. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode and then the ON mode. -3. Wait for at least 6 seconds. -4. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC B2648 Front Sensor Signal Short to Ground Is DTC B2648 indicated? YES Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for loose or poor connections.

-1. Clear the DTCs with the HDS.

-2. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode and then the ON mode.

-3. Wait for at least 6 seconds.

-4. Check for DTCs with the HDS.

B2648 Front Sensor Signal Short to Ground

Is DTC B2648 indicated?

YES

Go to step 2.

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for loose or poor connections.

- Shorted wire check (SOF SIDE L, E1 SIDE L lines) 1 -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the following connector. Parking and back-up sensor control unit 16P connector -3. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode Parking and back-up sensor control unit 16P connector: disconnected Test point 1 Parking and back-up sensor control unit 16P connector No. 6 Test point 2 Parking and back-up sensor control unit 16P connector No. 13 Is there 1 MΩ or more? YES Go to step 10. NO Go to step 3.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Disconnect the following connector.

Parking and back-up sensor control unit 16P connector

-3. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2.

Test point 1 | Parking and back-up sensor control unit 16P connector No. 6

Test point 2 | Parking and back-up sensor control unit 16P connector No. 13

Is there 1 MΩ or more?

YES

Go to step 10.

NO

Go to step 3.

- Shorted wire check (SOF SIDE R, E1 SIDE R lines) 1 -1. Disconnect the following connector. Right front center sensor 6P connector -2. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode Parking and back-up sensor control unit 16P connector: disconnected Right front center sensor 6P connector: disconnected Test point 1 Right front center sensor 6P connector No. 3 Test point 2 Right front center sensor 6P connector No. 1 Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there 1 MΩ or more? YES Go to step 5. NO Go to step 4.

-2. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2.

Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode Parking and back-up sensor control unit 16P connector: disconnected Right front center sensor 6P connector: disconnected Right front corner sensor 6P connector: disconnected Test point 1 Right front center sensor 6P connector No. 3 Test point 2 Right front center sensor 6P connector No. 1 Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there 1 MΩ or more? YES Replace the right front corner sensor . NO Repair a short in the SOF SIDE R and E1 SIDE R wires between the right front center sensor and the right front corner sensor.

# DTC U0155, U1281

DTC U0155 : Lost Communication with Gauge Control Module

DTC U1281 : Lost Communication with MICU (Body Control Module)

NOTE:

- If you are troubleshooting multiple DTCs, be sure to follow the instructions in B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A .

- According to the detected DTC(s), check for the power circuit and the ground circuit of the control unit which cannot communicate with the parking and back-up sensor control unit.

- Refer to the DTC shown on the display, then inspect the connectors and terminals based on the instructions.

DTC Description | DTC

U0155 Lost Communication with Gauge Control Module

U1281 Lost Communication with MICU (Body Control Module)

DTC (Parking and Back-Up Sensor Control Unit)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode and then the ON mode. -4. Wait for at least 6 seconds. -5. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC U0155 Lost Communication with Gauge Control Module U1281 Lost Communication with MICU (Body Control Module) Are any DTCs indicated? YES Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the transmitting control units and the parking and back-up sensor control unit.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode and then the ON mode.

-4. Wait for at least 6 seconds.

-5. Check for DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC

U0155 Lost Communication with Gauge Control Module

U1281 Lost Communication with MICU (Body Control Module)

Are any DTCs indicated?

YES

Go to step 2.

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the transmitting control units and the parking and back-up sensor control unit.

- Open wire check (B-CAN_H line, B-CAN_L line) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the receiving control unit connector. Parking and back-up sensor control unit 16P connector -3. Disconnect the transmitting control unit connector(s). Refer to the DTC shown on the display, then disconnect the connector(s) based on the instructions (see table). DTC Connector U0155 Gauge control module connector A (32P) U1281 Body control module connector B (36P) -4. Check for continuity between the receiving control unit and the transmitting control unit on the B-CAN_H circuit and the B-CAN_L circuit. Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode Parking and back-up sensor control unit 16P connector: disconnected Transmitting control unit connector(s) for indicated DTCs: disconnected DTC Circuit name Receiving control unit Transmitting control unit Connector Terminal Connector Terminal U0155 B-CAN_H Parking and back-up sensor control unit 16P connector No. 9 Gauge control module connector A (32P) No. 30 B-CAN_L No. 2 No. 31 U1281 B-CAN_H Parking and back-up sensor control unit 16P connector No. 9 Body control module connector B (36P) No. 20 B-CAN_L No. 2 No. 21 Is there 1.0 Ω or less? YES The B-CAN_H wire and the B-CAN_L wire are OK. Refer to the DTC shown on the display, then substitute or replace the control unit based on the instructions (see table). DTC Operation for transmitting control unit U0155 Substitute a known-good gauge control module , then recheck. If DTC U0155 goes away after substitution, replace the original gauge control module . U1281 Substitute a known-good body control module , then recheck. If DTC U1281 goes away after substitution, replace the original body control module . NO Repair an open in the B-CAN_H wire and/or the B-CAN_L wire between the parking and back-up sensor control unit and the transmitting control unit.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Disconnect the receiving control unit connector.

Parking and back-up sensor control unit 16P connector

-3. Disconnect the transmitting control unit connector(s).

Refer to the DTC shown on the display, then disconnect the connector(s) based on the instructions (see table).

DTC | Connector

U0155 | Gauge control module connector A (32P)

U1281 | Body control module connector B (36P)

-4. Check for continuity between the receiving control unit and the transmitting control unit on the B-CAN_H circuit and the B-CAN_L circuit.

# DTC B1003

DTC B1003 : BCM (Body Control Module) PROGRAM Error

NOTE:

- If you are troubleshooting multiple DTCs, be sure to follow the instructions in B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A .

- This DTC is indicated when a body control module update is not completed properly.

- Before updating the body control module, make sure the 12 volt battery in the vehicle is fully charged, and connect a 12 volt jumper battery (not a 12 volt battery charger) to maintain system voltage.

- Never turn the vehicle to the ACCESSORY mode or OFF (LOCK) mode, during the reprogramming. If there is a problem with the reprogramming, turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

DTC Description | DTC

B1003 BCM (Body Control Module) PROGRAM Error

DTC (Body Control Module)

# DTC B1032 (5-door) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC B1032 : CDS (Collision Detection Signal) Signal Error

NOTE: If you are troubleshooting multiple DTCs, be sure to follow the instructions in B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A .

DTC Description | DTC

B1032 CDS (Collision Detection Signal) Signal Error

DTC (Body Control Module)

- Problem verification -1. Clear the DTCs with the HDS. -2. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode and then the ON mode. -3. Wait for at least 6 seconds. -4. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC B1032 CDS (Collision Detection Signal) Signal Error Is DTC B1032 indicated? YES Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time.

-1. Clear the DTCs with the HDS.

-2. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode and then the ON mode.

-3. Wait for at least 6 seconds.

-4. Check for DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC

B1032 CDS (Collision Detection Signal) Signal Error

Is DTC B1032 indicated?

YES

Go to step 2.

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time.

- Open wire check (CDS line) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Do the 12 volt battery terminal disconnection procedure , and wait for at least 3 minutes. -3. Disconnect the following connectors. Body control module connector D (40P) SRS unit connector A (39P) -4. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode Body control module connector D (40P): disconnected SRS unit connector A (39P): disconnected Test point 1 Body control module connector D (40P) No. 34 Test point 2 SRS unit connector A (39P) No. 1 Is there continuity? YES Go to step 3. NO Repair an open or high resistance in the wire.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Do the 12 volt battery terminal disconnection procedure , and wait for at least 3 minutes.

-3. Disconnect the following connectors.

Body control module connector D (40P) SRS unit connector A (39P)

-4. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2.

Test condition | Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode Body control module connector D (40P): disconnected SRS unit connector A (39P): disconnected

Test point 1 | Body control module connector D (40P) No. 34

Test point 2 | SRS unit connector A (39P) No. 1

Is there continuity?

YES

Go to step 3.

NO

Repair an open or high resistance in the wire.

- Shorted wire check (CDS line) -1. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode Body control module connector D (40P): disconnected SRS unit connector A (39P): disconnected Test point 1 SRS unit connector A (39P) No. 1 Test point 2 Body ground Is there continuity? YES Repair a short to ground in the wire. NO The CDS wire is OK. Go to step 4.

-1. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2.

Test condition | Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode Body control module connector D (40P): disconnected SRS unit connector A (39P): disconnected

Test point 1 | SRS unit connector A (39P) No. 1

Test point 2 | Body ground

Is there continuity?

YES

Repair a short to ground in the wire.

NO

The CDS wire is OK. Go to step 4.

- Body control module check -1. Reconnect the following connector. Body control module connector D (40P) -2. Do the 12 volt battery terminal reconnection procedure . -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -4. Measure the voltage between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle ON mode SRS unit connector A (39P): disconnected Test point 1 Body control module connector D (40P) No. 34 Test point 2 Body ground Is there battery voltage? YES Replace the SRS unit . NO Replace the body control module .

-1. Reconnect the following connector.

Body control module connector D (40P)

-2. Do the 12 volt battery terminal reconnection procedure .

-3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-4. Measure the voltage between test points 1 and 2.

Test condition | Vehicle ON mode SRS unit connector A (39P): disconnected

Test point 1 | Body control module connector D (40P) No. 34

Test point 2 | Body ground

Is there battery voltage?

YES

Replace the SRS unit .

NO

Replace the body control module .
````

B1036 IG1 Power Supply Circuit Malfunction

DTC (Body Control Module)

- Problem verification -1. Clear the DTCs with the HDS. -2. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode and then the ON mode. -3. Wait for at least 6 seconds. -4. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC B1036 IG1 Power Supply Circuit Malfunction Is DTC B1036 indicated? YES Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for loose or poor connections.

-1. Clear the DTCs with the HDS.

-2. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode and then the ON mode.

-3. Wait for at least 6 seconds.

-4. Check for DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC

B1036 IG1 Power Supply Circuit Malfunction

YES

Go to step 2.

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for loose or poor connections.

- Fuse check -1. Check the following fuse. Fuse No. B7 (10 A) Location Under-dash fuse/relay box Is the fuse OK? YES Repair an open or high resistance in the wire between the under-dash fuse/relay box and the gauge control module. NO Replace the fuse. If the fuse blows again, repair a short to ground in the No. B7 (10 A) fuse circuit.

YES

NO

# DTC B1038 (With CAN gateway)

DTC B1038 : IMMOBILIZER F-CAN Authentication ID Not Received

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- According to the detected DTC(s), check for the power circuit and the ground circuit of the control unit which cannot communicate with the body control module.

- Refer to the DTC shown on the display, then inspect the connectors and terminals based on the instructions.

DTC Description | DTC

B1038 IMMOBILIZER F-CAN Authentication ID Not Received

DTC (Body Control Module)

- CAN gateway system DTC check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Check for CAN gateway system DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC U0029-00 CAN Gateway F-CAN ch A Bus Off U0047-00 CAN Gateway F-CAN ch B Bus Off U3000-49 CAN Gateway Internal Failure DTC (CAN GATEWAY) Is DTC U0029-00, U0047-00, and/or U3000-49 indicated? YES Go to the troubleshooting for CAN gateway system DTC(s) . NO Go to step 2.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Check for CAN gateway system DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC

U0029-00 CAN Gateway F-CAN ch A Bus Off

U0047-00 CAN Gateway F-CAN ch B Bus Off

U3000-49 CAN Gateway Internal Failure

DTC (CAN GATEWAY)

Is DTC U0029-00, U0047-00, and/or U3000-49 indicated?

YES

Go to the troubleshooting for CAN gateway system DTC(s) .

NO

Go to step 2.

- F-CAN circuit communication check -1. Select the FUNCTION TEST in the CAN gateway with the HDS, then select the F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check. F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check Does the body control module detect the CAN gateway Bus channels A? Bus A is Not Available Go to step 3. Bus A is Available Go to step 4.

-1. Select the FUNCTION TEST in the CAN gateway with the HDS, then select the F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check.

F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check

Does the body control module detect the CAN gateway Bus channels A?

Bus A is Not Available

Go to step 3.

Bus A is Available

Go to step 4.

- Open wire check (F-CAN A lines) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the following connectors. CAN gateway 12P connector *1 CAN gateway 16P connector *2 Body control module connector B (36P) *1: CAN gateway 12P connector type *2: CAN gateway 16P connector type -3. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2 respectively. CAN gateway 12P connector type Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode CAN gateway 12P connector: disconnected Body control module connector B (36P): disconnected Test point 1 CAN gateway 12P connector No. 3 Test point 2 Body control module connector B (36P) No. 22 Test point 1 CAN gateway 12P connector No. 9 Test point 2 Body control module connector B (36P) No. 23 Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. CAN gateway 16P connector type Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode CAN gateway 16P connector: disconnected Body control module connector B (36P): disconnected Test point 1 CAN gateway 16P connector No. 3 Test point 2 Body control module connector B (36P) No. 22 Test point 1 CAN gateway 16P connector No. 11 Test point 2 Body control module connector B (36P) No. 23 Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there 1.0 Ω or less? YES The F-CAN A_H and F-CAN A_L wires are OK. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the CAN gateway and the body control module. Substitute a known-good body control module , then recheck. If the DTC B1038 goes away and the body control module was substituted, replace the original body control module . NO Repair an open in F-CAN A_H or F-CAN A_L wire between the CAN gateway and the body control module.

-3. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2 respectively.
